WHAT A `keep` BUYS YOU. Anything you do not mark `delete` stays exactly where
you wrote it, on this run's storage volume, at the path it already has — it is
not moved, renamed or copied. A later round reads it there, by that absolute
workspace path, so a checkpoint you keep is a checkpoint the next round can
load instead of retraining. It is also the ONLY copy: the publish step pushes
your cwd to GitHub but skips every file of 100 MB or
more, so trained weights and large binary artifacts never leave the volume.

<artifact_info>
id: art_HF8nK8B_Lqyy
type: experiment
title: Did post-training cause Qwen3's algorithm collapse?
summary: >-
  Screen of alternate 4 ('instruction tuning installed the algorithm-choice collapse') on Qwen3-8B-Base vs Qwen3-8B (thinking
  off), two path-search tasks: T1 unweighted grid path, T2 weighted directed shortest path. 4140 programs, each run on 47/46
  hidden tests and labelled by a runtime-signature + AST labeller with a blind LLM judge (gemini-2.5-flash-lite) for disagreements
  (reference gate: AST 100%, runtime never wrong; kappa runtime-AST 0.77; inter-judge kappa 0.93). 2x2 weights x format (T=1,
  top-p 1): instruct chat is collapsed (T1 BFS 200/200, Hill_cc 1.00; T2 Dijkstra 193/200, Hill_cc 1.16). The base model under
  the same chat tokens is more diverse (T1 BFS 49/57 correct, Hill_cc 1.58; T2 Dijkstra 63/90, Hill_cc 2.09); template-free
  base code is mostly broken (pass 5%/8%; T2 correct programs Bellman-Ford-modal 16/25). D_weights = Hill_cc(instruct_chat)
  - Hill_cc(base_chat) = -0.58 [-1.01,-0.15] (T1), -0.93 [-1.38,-0.46] (T2). Pre-registered verdicts: T2 INSTALLED_BY_POST_TRAINING;
  T1 MIXED (modal-share gap 14.0 pp, just under the 15 pp bar); no ALREADY_IN_BASE, so the alternate passes the screen (also
  under Hill_all). At the planned N (before the N=300 base extension) both read MIXED. Post-training concentrated the base
  model's existing mode rather than inventing one; the chat format also concentrates the instruct model on T2 (template-free
  Hill_cc 1.93 -> chat 1.16) and switches the base model's T2 mode from Bellman-Ford to Dijkstra. Mechanistic test: the paired
  instruct-minus-base residual difference at the read position (split-half cos 0.998, task-general cos 0.85-0.92, logit lens
  = answer-format tokens) subtracted over layers 8-28 is DESTRUCTIVE at c=0.5/1.0/1.5 (0/100 parseable), while norm-matched
  random directions keep format and algorithm; downstream layers amplify the subtraction (overshoot, gap_closed ~0 at L18/L28).
  Single-layer delta_18 fallback: c=0.25 keeps 94-98% pass with zero algorithm change (NO_EFFECT). R-only subtraction flips
  Qwen3 into thinking-style prose yet stated plans and correct programs keep BFS/Dijkstra. No knob restores diversity; the
  ban prompt does better (T2 Hill_cc 1.16 -> 1.99 at 0 pp pass loss). Hash-committed forecast from the base model hit top-1
  on both ban events (DFS; Bellman-Ford), log score -0.47/-0.22 vs uniform -1.63/-1.41, but base-chat/typicality references
  did as well. All headline numbers were re-derived by an independent script (0 mismatches; placebo permutation tests reject
  at ~5%). Reusable outputs: runs/samples.jsonl (shared row schema, every sample), runs/labelled_*.jsonl, method_out.json
  (metadata = all analyses: collapse_screen, steering, rows.BUY / rows.PREDICT, labeller_qa, sharpening_direction), data/
  (prompts, hidden tests, references), src/ labeller + harness, activations/ (residual states, deltas, band steering vectors;
  kept on the run volume), predictions/ (committed forecast).

<data_loading_pattern>
`mini_demo_data.json` = curated subset for the demo.
Use this pattern for Colab compatibility (GitHub URL with local fallback):
```python
GITHUB_DATA_URL = "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ai-inventor-papers/ai-invention-295f8e-prompt-bans-substitute-not-just-subtract/fork/run_jSassd-YWLVC/round-1/experiment-5/demo/mini_demo_data.json"
import http.client
import json
import urllib.request
from pathlib import Path

def load_data():
    try:
        with urllib.request.urlopen(GITHUB_DATA_URL, timeout=15) as response:
            return json.loads(response.read().decode())
    # Network errors, timeouts, a placeholder URL, bad JSON, a cut-off response.
    except (OSError, ValueError, http.client.HTTPException) as e:
        print("GitHub data unavailable, using local mini_demo_data.json:", e)
    local = Path("mini_demo_data.json")
    if local.exists(): return json.loads(local.read_text())
    raise FileNotFoundError("Could not load mini_demo_data.json")
```
</data_loading_pattern>

take this repo as inspiration: https://github.com/DrejcPesjak/llm-creativity
use one or more open-weight models (minimum 1B params), probably instruction tuned it
and first run behavioral analysis (similar to repo mentioned above), then move to interpretability (can be manifolds, or also other techniques like sae, circuits, nla, j-lens, logit lens...)
the question is: how does "creativity" manifest inside the model, and can we influence it?
(in layman terms, can we make llms give truly creative and novel solutions?)

(you do not need to implement everything bellow, see whats reasonable, interesting and might lead to new insights)
is "temp" really the "llm creativity knob"?  (Astra found "coincidentally" similar direction for navier-stokes solution, as Buckmaster & Levent)
(always same seed)
1 a) does a tight temp , with topk=10 produce 10 reasoning trajectories of 1 idea/solution but same 10 phrasings
b) or does tight temp, with topk=10 produce 10 reasoning trajectories of 10 ideas/solutions with 1 phrasing each
c) and does loosing up temp, make a) more to 2/3/4 ideas each with 10 phrasings ; or make b) keep original 10 ideas, now each with 10 phrasings
(temp is rather a minor part in this idea, put more focus on the following things)
test: "implement path saerch algorithm" -> does llm always design BFS?
2 can context seeding help? prepand "seed=d32qxk4nt43" or "seed=(horse chair roof love)"
hyp: is llm a probablistic model, and thus gives you the average/mean answer? and only context pushes you into a different subspace
 - ie of possible range of answer [0,100] , on direct question llm gives you 50. then with context you move to [80,100] subspace and llm gives you 90
3 so how much does prompt instructions matter? ("Make a new algorithm", "Make a fully novel algorithm never seen before ... 2000 words ..."
-> analyze the internals , all the runs that produced BFS , vs all runs that produced DFS , vs dijkstra ...
  -> analyze with thought anchors, NLA, SAE ...
  -> what is the average answer? plot a "path search algorithm" manifold, color each algorithm centroid by implemented-count (lets say we have 10 algorithms in total of 100 gens)
     -> then prohibit the top 5 in prompt, and see if the implemented count moves to the edge of those 5, or to the opposite side (if its a circle manifold)
  - (manifold lit: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.05115 , https://www.goodfire.com/research/manifold-steering ) - geodesic and mds
    - (the reports need to contain actual geometry plots, actual activation space, not some summary bar charts)

return out\n\n\ndef write_jsonl(p: Path, rows: Iterable[dict]) -> None:\n    tmp = p.with_suffix(p.suffix + \".tmp\")\n    with open(tmp, \"w\") as f:\n        for r in rows:\n            f.write(json.dumps(r) + \"\\n\")\n    tmp.replace(p)\n\n\ndef append_jsonl(p: Path, rows: Iterable[dict]) -> None:\n    with open(p, \"a\") as f:\n        for r in rows:\n tuple[float, float, float]:\n    if n == 0:\n        return (float(\"nan\"), float(\"nan\"), float(\"nan\"))\n    p = k / n\n    den = 1 + z * z / n\n    c = (p + z * z / (2 * n)) / den\n    h = z * math.sqrt(p * (1 - p) / n + z * z / (4 * n * n)) / den\n    return (p, max(0.0, c - h), min(1.0, c + h))\n\n\ndef time_log(event: str, **kw: Any) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Append a phase-boundary event to lab in cats else \"other\"\n\n\ndef hill(counts: np.ndarray) -> float:\n    n = counts.sum()\n    if n <= 0:\n        return float(\"nan\")\n    p = counts[counts > 0] / n\n    return float(math.exp(-(p * np.log(p)).sum()))\n\n\ndef js(p_counts: np.ndarray, q_counts: np.ndarray, smooth: float = 0.5) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Jensen-Shannon divergence (nats). smooth=0 gives the unsmoothed JS (finite: zero cells contribute 0).\"\"\"\n    p = (p_counts + smooth) / (p_counts + smooth).sum()\n    q = (q_counts + smooth) / (q_counts + smooth).sum()\n    m = 0.5 * (p + q)\n\n    def kl(a: np.ndarray) -> float:\n        nz = a > 0\n        return float((a[nz] * np.log(a[nz] / m[nz])).sum())\n    return 0.5 * kl(p) + 0.5 * kl(q)\n\n\nclass ArmData:\n    \"\"\"Per-arm arrays: [\"other\"]\n        self.K = len(self.cats)\n        idx = {c: i for i, c in enumerate(self.cats)}\n        self.pid = np.array([r[\"paraphrase_id\"] for r in rows], dtype=int)\n        self.sid = np.array([r[\"sample_id\"] for r in rows], dtype=int)\n        labs = []\n        for r in rows:\n            lab = r.get(label_key)\n            if not r.get(\"parse_ok\") or lab is None:\n                labs.append(-1)\n            else:\n                labs.append(idx[norm_label(lab, cats)])\n        self.lab = np.array(labs, dtype=int)\n        self.cor = np.array([bool(r.get(\"passed\")) for r in rows], dtype=bool)\n        self.n = len(rows)\n        self.groups = {p: np.where(self.pid == p)[0] for p in np.unique(self.pid)} if self.n else {}\n\n    def counts(self, idx: np.ndarray | None = None, correct_only: bool = True) -> np.ndarray:\n        lab = self.lab if idx is None else self.lab[idx]\n        cor = self.cor if idx is None else self.cor[idx]\n        m = (lab >= 0) & (cor if correct_only else True)\n        return np.bincount(lab[m], minlength=self.K)\n\n    def resample_idx(self, rng: np.random.Generator, pids: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n        parts = []\n        for p in pids:\n            g = self.groups.get(p)\n            if g is None or len(g) == 0:\n                continue\n            parts.append(rng.choice(g, size=len(g), replace=True))\n        return np.concatenate(parts) if parts else np.array([], dtype=int)\n\n\ndef boot_stat(arms: list[ArmData], fn, B: int = 2000, seed: int = 0, min_correct: int = 5) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Paraphrase-cluster bootstrap; paraphrase ids are resampled JOINTLY across the given arms.\n    fn(list of index arrays) -> float (nan allowed). Resamples where any arm has < min_correct correct samples are skipped.\"\"\"\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    all_p = np.unique(np.concatenate([np.array(list(a.groups.keys())) for a in arms if a.n])) if arms else np.array([])\n    vals, skipped = [], 0\n    for _ in range(B):\n        pids = rng.choice(all_p, size=len(all_p), replace=True)\n        idxs = [a.resample_idx(rng, pids) for a in arms]\n        if any(int((a.cor[ix] & (a.lab[ix] >= 0)).sum()) < min_correct for a, ix in zip(arms, idxs)):\n            skipped += 1\n            continue\n        v = fn(idxs)\n        if v is None or (isinstance(v, float) and math.isnan(v)):\n            skipped += 1\n            continue\n        vals.append(v)\n    full = fn([np.arange(a.n) for a in arms])\n    if not vals:\n        return {\"est\": full, \"lo\": None, \"hi\": None, \"n_boot\": 0, \"skipped\": skipped}\n    lo, hi = np.percentile(vals, [2.5, 97.5])\n    return {\"est\": full, \"lo\": float(lo), \"hi\": float(hi), \"n_boot\": len(vals), \"skipped\": skipped}\n\n\ndef perm_chi2(a_labels: np.ndarray, b_labels: np.ndarray, K: int, n_perm: int = 10000, seed: int = 0) -> dict:\n    a_labels = a_labels[a_labels >= 0]\n    b_labels = b_labels[b_labels >= 0]\n    if len(a_labels) == 0 or len(b_labels) == 0:\n        return {\"chi2\": None, \"p\": None, \"n_a\": int(len(a_labels)), \"n_b\": int(len(b_labels))}\n    pooled = np.concatenate([a_labels, b_labels])\n    na = len(a_labels)\n\n    def chi2(arr: np.ndarray) -> float:\n        ca = np.bincount(arr[:na], minlength=K).astype(float)\n        cb = np.bincount(arr[na:], minlength=K).astype(float)\n        tot = ca + cb\n        keep = tot > 0\n        ca, cb, tot = ca[keep], cb[keep], tot[keep]\n        ea = tot * na / len(arr)\n        eb = tot * (len(arr) - na) / len(arr)\n        return float(((ca - ea) ** 2 / ea).sum() + ((cb - eb) ** 2 / eb).sum())\n\n    obs = chi2(pooled)\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    ge = 0\n    for _ in range(n_perm):\n        if chi2(rng.permutation(pooled)) >= obs - 1e-12:\n            ge += 1\n    return {\"chi2\": obs, \"p\": (ge + 1) / (n_perm + 1), \"n_a\": int(na), \"n_b\": int(len(b_labels))}\n\n\ndef cohen_kappa(a: list[str], b: list[str]) -> float | None:\n    if not a:\n        return None\n    cats = sorted(set(a) | set(b))\n    idx = {c: i for i, c in enumerate(cats)}\n    M = np.zeros((len(cats), len(cats)))\n    for x, y in zip(a, b):\n        M[idx[x], idx[y]] += 1\n    n = M.sum()\n    po = np.trace(M) / n\n    pe = (M.sum(0) * M.sum(1)).sum() / (n * n)\n    return float((po - pe) / (1 - pe)) if pe < 1 else 1.0", "stderr": "", "interrupted": false, "isImage": false, "noOutputExpected": false}

dict]]:\n    ib = {(r[\"paraphrase_id\"], r[\"sample_id\"]): r for r in rows_b}\n    return [(ra, ib[(ra[\"paraphrase_id\"], ra[\"sample_id\"])]) for ra in rows_a if (ra[\"paraphrase_id\"], ra[\"sample_id\"]) in ib]\n\n\ndef flip_stats(pairs: list[tuple[dict, dict]], cats: list[str]) -> dict:\n    both = [(a, b) for a, b in pairs if a.get(\"parse_ok\") and b.get(\"parse_ok\") and a.get(\"label\") and b.get(\"label\")]\n    if not both:\n        return {\"n_pairs_both_parse\": 0, \"flip_rate\": None, \"transitions\": {}}\n    trans: dict = {}\n    flips = 0\n    for a, b in both:\n        la, lb = norm_label(a[\"label\"], cats), norm_label(b[\"label\"], cats)\n        trans.setdefault(lb, {}).setdefault(la, 0)\n        trans[lb][la] += 1\n        flips += la != lb\n    return {\"n_pairs_both_parse\": len(both), \"flip_rate\": flips / len(both), \"transitions_from_S0_to_arm\": trans}\n\n\ndef flip_boot(pairs_by_arm: dict[str, list], cats: list[str], arm: str, rnd: list[str], seed: int = 31) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"flip(arm) - mean flip(random arms), paired bootstrap over paraphrases.\"\"\"\n    def fr(pairs):\n        both = [(a, b) for a, b in pairs if a.get(\"parse_ok\") and b.get(\"parse_ok\") and a.get(\"label\") and b.get(\"label\")]\n        if not both:\n            return None\n        return sum(norm_label(a[\"label\"], cats) != norm_label(b[\"label\"], cats) for a, b in both) / len(both)\n    if arm not in pairs_by_arm or not all(r in pairs_by_arm for r in rnd):\n        return {\"est\": None}\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    pids = sorted({a[\"paraphrase_id\"] for a, _ in pairs_by_arm[arm]})\n    vals = []\n\n    def stat(sel):\n        f1 = fr([p for p in pairs_by_arm[arm] if p[0][\"paraphrase_id\"] in sel])\n        fr_r = [fr([p for p in pairs_by_arm[r] if p[0][\"paraphrase_id\"] in sel]) for r in rnd]\n        if f1 is None or any(x is None for x in fr_r):\n            return None\n        return f1 - float(np.mean(fr_r))\n    est = stat(set(pids))\n    for _ in range(1000):\n        ch = rng.choice(pids, size=len(pids), replace=True)\n        # cluster bootstrap with multiplicity: expand pairs per chosen paraphrase\n        def expand(pairs):\n            out = []\n            for p in ch:\n                out.extend([x for x in pairs if x[0][\"paraphrase_id\"] == p])\n            return out\n        f1 = fr(expand(pairs_by_arm[arm]))\n        fr_r = [fr(expand(pairs_by_arm[r])) for r in rnd]\n        if f1 is None or any(x is None for x in fr_r):\n            continue\n        vals.append(f1 - float(np.mean(fr_r)))\n    if not vals:\n        return {\"est\": est, \"lo\": None, \"hi\": None}\n    lo, hi = np.percentile(vals, [2.5, 97.5])\n    return {\"est\": est, \"lo\": float(lo), \"hi\": float(hi), \"n_boot\": len(vals)}\n\n\ndef steering(task: str, arms: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> dict:\n    cats = CATALOGUE[task]\n logged[-1][\"sha256\"] if logged else None\n    ev = pred[\"events\"][task]\n    S = ev[\"survivors\"]\n    rows_c = task_rows(arms[\"instruct_chat_ban\"], task)\n    comp = [r for r in rows_c if else \"other\" for l in labs]\n    cats = S + [\"other\"]\n    cnt = np.array([labs.count(c) for c in cats], dtype=float)\n    realised = None\n    if cnt.sum():\n        order = np.argsort(-cnt)\n        lead = (cnt[order[0]] - (cnt[order[1]] if len(order) > 1 else 0)) / cnt.sum()\n        realised = cats[order[0]] if lead >= 0.15 else \"no clear mode\"\n    out = []\n    for name, pr in ev[\"predictors\"].items():\n        p = pr[\"p\"]\n        ls = float(np.mean([math.log(p[l]) for l in labs])) if labs else None\n        pvec = np.array([p[c] for c in cats])\n        jsd = None\n        if cnt.sum():\n            q = cnt / cnt.sum()\n            m = 0.5 * (pvec + q)\n            jsd = float(0.5 * (pvec * np.log(pvec / m)).sum() + 0.5 * sum(qi * math.log(qi / mi) for qi, mi in zip(q, m) if qi > 0))\n        out.append({\"task\": task, \"event\": ev[\"event\"], \"predictor\": name, \"primary\": name == ev[\"primary\"],\n                    \"sha256_committed\": sha_committed, \"sha256_now\": sha_now, \"hash_match\": sha_committed == sha_now,\n                    \"n_samples\": len(rows_c), \"n_compliant\": len(comp), \"realised_counts\": {c: int(x) for c, x in zip(cats, cnt)},\n                    \"realised_mode\": realised, \"top1\": pr[\"top1\"],\n                    \"top1_hit\": (realised == pr[\"top1\"]) if realised not in (None, \"no clear mode\") else False,\n                    \"log_score\": ls, \"js_to_realised\": jsd, \"flags\": ev.get(\"flags\", [])})\n    return out\n\n\ndef ban_obedience(task: str, arms: dict) -> dict:\n    if \"base_tf_ban\" not in arms or \"base_tf\" not in arms:\n        return {}\n    cats = CATALOGUE[task]\n    a3 = task_rows(arms[\"base_tf_ban\"], task)\n    a1 = task_rows(arms[\"base_tf\"], task)\n    ban = BANNED[task]\n\n    def share(rows, correct_only):\n        labs = [norm_label(r[\"label\"], cats) for r in rows if r.get(\"parse_ok\") and r.get(\"label\") and (r.get(\"passed\") or not correct_only)]\n        return (sum(l == ban for l in labs), len(labs))\n    out = {\"banned_mode\": ban}\n    for co in (False, True):\n        k3, n3 = share(a3, co)\n        k1, n1 = share(a1, co)\n        tag = \"correct\" if co else \"parseable\"\n        out[f\"banned_share_ban_{tag}\"] = wilson(k3, n3)\n        out[f\"banned_share_baseline_{tag}\"] = wilson(k1, n1)\n        # bootstrap CI on the difference (resample samples within arm)\n        rng = np.random.default_rng(5)\n        l3 = np.array([norm_label(r[\"label\"], cats) == ban for r in a3 if r.get(\"parse_ok\") and r.get(\"label\") and (r.get(\"passed\") or not co)], dtype=float)\n        l1 = np.array([norm_label(r[\"label\"], cats) == ban for r in a1 if r.get(\"parse_ok\") and r.get(\"label\") and (r.get(\"passed\") or not co)], dtype=float)\n        if len(l3) and len(l1):\n            d = [rng.choice(l3, len(l3)).mean() - rng.choice(l1, len(l1)).mean() for _ in range(2000)]\n            out[f\"diff_ban_minus_baseline_{tag}\"] = {\"est\": float(l3.mean() - l1.mean()), \"lo\": float(np.percentile(d, 2.5)),\n                                                    \"hi\": float(np.percentile(d, 97.5))}\n    comp = [r for r in a3 if r.get(\"passed\") and r.get(\"label\") and norm_label(r[\"label\"], cats) != ban]\n    out[\"compliance_rate\"] = wilson(len(comp), (Qwen3-8B-Base vs Qwen3-8B)\n\nScreen of **alternate 4 — \"instruction tuning installed the collapse\"** on two path-search coding tasks:\n\n* **T1** unweighted grid path (`solve(grid, start, goal)`, any valid path) — catalogue: `bfs, dfs, bidir_bfs, iddfs, astar_greedy, dijkstra_unit`\n* **T2** weighted directed shortest path (`solve(n, edges, s, t)`) — catalogue: `dijkstra, bellman_ford_spfa, bidir_dijkstra, floyd_warshall, dial`\n\nEvery generated program is executed on ≥44 hidden tests per task (47 / 46 cases, per-case wall-clock limit = max(1 s, 10× reference)),\nand labelled by a **runtime-signature labeller** (instrumented heaps/deques/list pops, recursion depth, grid read order, edge-list passes,\nline-count scaling) plus an **AST labeller**; when they disagree a **blind LLM judge** (`google/gemini-2.5-flash-lite`, sees only spec +\ncatalogue + code) decides. Decoding everywhere: T = 1.0, top-p 1.0, no top-k, thinking off, max 1024 new tokens.\n\n## What was done\n\n1. **2×2 weights × format decomposition** (vLLM 0.11, bf16):\n   `base_tf` (Qwen3-8B-Base, template-free docstring prompt), `base_chat` (base model on the *instruct* chat template),\n   `instruct_chat` (Qwen3-8B chat, `enable_thinking=False`), `instruct_tf` (instruct model on the template-free prompt);\n   10 neutral paraphrases (P0..P9). Same task text, same sampling, token-identical chat prompts across tokenizers (asserted for all 120).\n2. **Modal-ban arms**: `base_tf_ban` (A3) and `instruct_chat_ban` (C1) with 10 ban phrasings (ban:BFS on T1, ban:Dijkstra on T2).\n3. **Hash-committed forecast (PREDICT)** of where the instruct model lands under the ban, made from the base model's template-free\n   distribution and committed (sha256 + git) **before** C1 was sampled; scored by top-1 hit and log score against uniform / typicality references.\n4. **Sharpening direction**: δ_L = mean over 50 paraphrases of h_R(instruct) − h_R(base) on identical chat-templated tokens (R = last prompt\n   token), every layer; diagnostics (split-half reliability, task generality, massive-activation share, cos with teacher-forced generated-position\n   difference, logit lens).\n5. **Steering** (HF, custom common-random-number sampler): band-incremental subtraction over decoder layers 8–28 (cumulative shift after layer L\n   = c·δ_L) at R and at every generated position, c ∈ {0.5, 1.0, 1.5} with a hooked c = 0 control; cumulative-norm-matched random directions at c*;\n   per-sample flips on coupled seeds; manipulation check (gap closed at R). Plan fallback 6 (single-layer δ₁₈ at layer 18) and an extension\n   ablation (**R-only**: subtract only at R, not at generated positions, with an R-only random control).\n\n## Layout\n......\n## Results\n\nAll numbers below are reproduced from `results/analysis.json` (tables in the next section are generated by `src/report.py`).\nCIs are 95 %: Wilson for proportions, paraphrase-cluster bootstrap (B = 2000, paraphrases resampled jointly across arms) for Hill numbers.\n\n### Did post-training install the collapse? Pre-registered verdicts: T2 INSTALLED_BY_POST_TRAINING, T1 MIXED (narrowly) → alternate 4 passes the screen\n\n| | T1 grid path (correct programs) | T2 weighted shortest path (correct programs) |\n|---|---|---|\n| base, template-free (N=300) | BFS 12/15, DFS 3 — Hill_cc 1.65 [1.00, 2.00] | **Bellman-Ford 16/25**, Dijkstra 8, FW 1 — Hill_cc 2.18 [1.00, 2.73] |\n| base, chat template (N=300) | BFS 49/57, DFS 7, A* 1 — Hill_cc 1.58 [1.10, 2.06] | **Dijkstra 63/90**, Bellman-Ford 24, FW 1, other 2 — Hill_cc 2.09 [1.63, 2.54] |\n| instruct, template-free (N=100) | BFS 73/73 — Hill_cc 1.00 | Dijkstra 57/72, FW 8, Bellman-Ford 7 — Hill_cc 1.93 [1.41, 2.37] |\n| instruct, chat template (N=200) | **BFS 200/200** — Hill_cc 1.00 | **Dijkstra 193/200**, Bellman-Ford 7 — Hill_cc 1.16 [1.03, 1.31] |\n\n* **Weights (same chat tokens, base vs instruct weights):** D_weights = Hill_cc(instruct_chat) − Hill_cc(base_chat) = **−0.58 [−1.01, −0.15]**\n  (T1) and **−0.93 [−1.38, −0.46]** (T2); label distributions differ (permutation χ² p = 0.0001 on both). The modal-share gap is 26.5 pp on\n  T2 (bar ≥ 15 pp met → **INSTALLED_BY_POST_TRAINING**) but only **14.0 pp on T1** (instruct 100 % BFS vs base-chat 86 % BFS), so T1 misses\n  the pre-registered bar on the modal-share criterion alone and reads **MIXED**. No task reads ALREADY_IN_BASE, so the alternate passes the\n  screen. Under the Hill_all co-primary (fallback 3) both tasks read INSTALLED_BY_POST_TRAINING.\n* **What post-training did is concentrate an existing mode, not invent one.** The instruct model's mode is already the base model's modal\n  choice on T1 (BFS in both formats) and on T2 under the chat template (Dijkstra 70 %). Post-training removed the minority modes\n  (T1: DFS 12 % → 0 %; T2: Bellman-Ford 27 % → 3.5 %).\n* **Format matters too, and differently for the two models.** Within base weights the chat template does not change Hill_cc significantly\n  (T1 −0.07 [−0.76, 0.88]; T2 −0.09 [−0.79, 1.12]) but on T2 it switches the base model's *mode* from Bellman-Ford (template-free, 64 %) to\n  Dijkstra (70 %; permutation p = 0.0044). Within instruct weights the chat template further collapses T2 (Hill_cc 1.93 → 1.16,\n  D = −0.76 [−1.26, −0.23]); T1 is already fully collapsed in both formats.\n* **Robustness.** Judge labels instead of final labels: same verdicts (T2 D_weights −1.02 [−1.51, −0.51]). At the *planned* sample sizes\n  (before the N = 300 power extension; 19–23 correct base-chat programs per task) both tasks read MIXED (CIs reach 0) — the T2 verdict needs\n  the extension. Base template-free code is mostly broken (pass 5 % / 8 %), so base Hill_cc rests on 15 / 25 correct programs.\n* **Priors (context only):** Invisible Lottery Table 12 reports Dijkstra 94.1 % (no cue, T = 0) for T2-like prompts; the inspiration repo\n  reports Dijkstra 25/25 — consistent with the instruct chat arm here (96.5 %).\n\n### Bans, the hash-committed forecast, and the prompting baseline\n\n* **Instruct model under the modal ban (C1):** ban:BFS is mostly ignored — 57 of 70 correct T1 programs are still BFS; the 13 compliant ones\n  land on DFS (12) and Dijkstra-unit (1). ban:Dijkstra is obeyed by 46 % (all correct), landing 46/46 on Bellman-Ford.\n* **Forecast (committed 2026-10-19T17:15:56Z, sha256 `94854c80…d4d47`, git `908b4ee`, before C1 existed; hash re-verified at analysis):**\n  primary predictor (base template-free distribution restricted to survivors, fallback 3) **hit top-1 on both events** (DFS; Bellman-Ford),\n  log score −0.47 (T1, n = 13 compliant) and −0.22 (T2, n = 46) vs uniform −1.63 / −1.41. The base-chat predictor and the instruct\n  typicality reference score as well or better on T2 (−0.058), so the hit shows the substitution target is shared by base and instruct\n  models rather than uniquely predicted by the base distribution.\n* **Base model ignores bans (A3):** banned-mode share among parseable template-free programs 0.36 vs 0.46 (T1, diff −0.10 [−0.21, 0.01])\n  and 0.27 vs 0.25 (T2); correct-and-compliant rate 0 % (T1) / 4 % (T2).\n* **BUY (diversity gained per pass-rate point lost):** the *prompt* is by far the best knob — ban:Dijkstra lifts T2 Hill_cc 1.16 → 1.99 at\n  0 pp pass loss (efficiency 0.83); ban:BFS lifts T1 1.00 → 1.70 at 30 pp loss. No sharpening setting gains any diversity (Hill 2 and 3\n  \"not reached within the tested range\"); AxBench's prompting-beats-steering pattern holds here.\n\n### Mechanistic test: does removing the instruct-minus-base read state undo the collapse? ### [45] TOOL CALL — Write · 2026-10-26 09:14:16 UTC

```
File: /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_1/art_HF8nK8B_Lqyy/make_mini_demo_data.py

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Build mini_demo_data.json for code_demo.ipynb from the experiment's workspace (read-only).

ONE dataset (T2 weighted directed shortest path), 100 labelled programs spread over the five vLLM arms that the CPU part
of the analysis needs: the 2x2 weights-by-format arms (base_tf, base_chat, instruct_tf, instruct_chat) plus the
Dijkstra-ban arm (instruct_chat_ban). Alongside the rows it stores the small result / prediction files that method.py's
assemble() and the analysis step read from disk, and the full-run numbers (results/analysis.json) for comparison.

  python make_mini_demo_data.py --src <experiment workspace> [--out mini_demo_data.json]
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import math
import random
from collections import Counter, defaultdict
from pathlib import Path

TASK = "t2"
DATASET = "T2_weighted_shortest_path"
T2_LABELS = ["dijkstra", "bellman_ford_spfa", "bidir_dijkstra", "floyd_warshall", "dial"]
ARM_FILES = {
    "base_tf": "labelled_base_tf__baseline.jsonl",
    "base_chat": "labelled_base_chat__baseline.jsonl",
    "instruct_tf": "labelled_instruct_tf__baseline.jsonl",
    "instruct_chat": "labelled_instruct_chat__baseline.jsonl",
    "instruct_chat_ban": "labelled_instruct_chat__ban.jsonl",
}
# Per arm: correct programs to take per algorithm label, and how many failing programs to add.
# Correct-label quotas keep each arm's correct-program mix close to the full run, so the correct-only Hill numbers
# (the pre-registered primary statistic) are meaningful at n = 11..20 per arm. Failing programs are sampled
# proportionally to the arm's failing-label mix. Passing programs are therefore OVER-represented in the base arms
# (full-run pass rates: base_tf 8 %, base_chat 30 %), so demo pass rates are not representative.
QUOTAS = {
    "base_tf": ({"bellman_ford_spfa": 8, "dijkstra": 4, "floyd_warshall": 1}, 12),
    "base_chat": ({"dijkstra": 10, "bellman_ford_spfa": 3, "other": 1}, 11),
    "instruct_tf": ({"dijkstra": 9, "floyd_warshall": 1, "bellman_ford_spfa": 1}, 4),
    "instruct_chat": ({"dijkstra": 19, "bellman_ford_spfa": 1}, 0),
    "instruct_chat_ban": ({"dijkstra": 8, "bellman_ford_spfa": 7}, 0),
}
JSON_FILES = ["results/gen_meta_A.json", "results/gen_meta_B.json", "results/gen_meta_C.json", "results/gen_meta_A_EXT.json",
              "results/deviations.json", "results/sharpening_direction.json", "results/harness_calibration.json",
              "results/hook_tests.json", "results/special_token_norms_base.json", "results/special_token_norms_instruct.json",
              "results/judge_cost.json", "results/predict_guard_test.json", "results/labeller_gate.json",
              "results/manipulation_S.json", "results/manipulation_R.json", "results/manipulation_X.json",
              "data/tests/t2.json", "time_log.json"]
# kept byte-exact: predict_rows() re-hashes the committed forecast and compares it with hash_log.txt
TEXT_FILES = ["predictions/predict_modal_ban.json", "predictions/hash_log.txt", "predictions/git_commit.txt"]


def clean(x):
    """Strict JSON: NaN/inf -> None (same rule as method.py's clean())."""
    if isinstance(x, float):
        return None if (math.isnan(x) or math.isinf(x)) else x
    if isinstance(x, dict):
        return {str(k): clean(v) for k, v in x.items()}
    if isinstance(x, (list, tuple)):
        return [clean(v) for v in x]
    return x


def group(r: dict) -> str:
    lab = r.get("label")
    if not r.get("parse_ok") or lab is None:
        return "unparseable"
    return lab if lab in T2_LABELS else "other"


def proportional(counts: dict, n: int) -> dict:
    """Largest-remainder split of n over the keys of counts."""
    tot = sum(counts.values())
    raw = {k: n * v / tot for k, v in counts.items()}
    q = {k: int(x) for k, x in raw.items()}
    for k in sorted(raw, key=lambda k: (-(raw[k] - q[k]), k))[: n - sum(q.values())]:
        q[k] += 1
    return {k: v for k, v in q.items() if v}


def pick(rows: list[dict], n: int, rng: random.Random) -> list[dict]:
    """n rows, round-robin over paraphrases so that every prompt paraphrase is represented."""
    by_p = defaultdict(list)
    for r in sorted(rows, key=lambda r: (r["paraphrase_id"], r["sample_id"])):
        by_p[r["paraphrase_id"]].append(r)
    for v in by_p.values():
        rng.shuffle(v)
    pids = sorted(by_p)
    rng.shuffle(pids)
    out: list[dict] = []
    while len(out) < n and any(by_p[p] for p in pids):
        for p in pids:
            if by_p[p] and len(out) < n:
                out.append(by_p[p].pop())
    if len(out) < n:
        raise SystemExit(f"stratum has only {len(out)} rows, {n} requested")
    return out


def interleave(strata: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> list[dict]:
    """Order rows so that every prefix is (approximately) stratified: ROWS_PER_ARM in the notebook truncates a prefix."""
    keyed = []
    for name, rows in strata.items():
        for i, r in enumerate(rows):
            keyed.append(((i + 0.5) / len(rows), name, r["paraphrase_id"], r["sample_id"], r))
    return [k[-1] for k in sorted(keyed, key=lambda k: k[:4])]


def main() -> None:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--src", required=True, type=Path, help="experiment workspace (contains runs/, results/, data/)")
    ap.add_argument("--out", default=Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "mini_demo_data.json", type=Path)
    ap.add_argument("--seed", default=0, type=int)
    a = ap.parse_args()
    src = a.src
    rng = random.Random(a.seed)

    files: dict[str, dict] = {}
    per_arm: dict[str, dict] = {}
    for arm, fname in ARM_FILES.items():
        rows = [json.loads(x) for x in (src / "runs" / fname).read_text().splitlines() if x.strip()]
        rows = [r for r in rows if r["task"] == TASK]
        correct_q, n_fail = QUOTAS[arm]
        strata: dict[str, list[dict]] = {}
        for lab, n in correct_q.items():
            strata[f"pass:{lab}"] = pick([r for r in rows if r.get("passed") and group(r) == lab], n, rng)
        failing = [r for r in rows if not r.get("passed")]
        if n_fail:
            for lab, n in proportional(Counter(group(r) for r in failing), n_fail).items():
                strata[f"fail:{lab}"] = pick([r for r in failing if group(r) == lab], n, rng)
        chosen = interleave(strata)
        files[f"runs/{fname}"] = {"kind": "jsonl", "content": chosen}
        full_cc = Counter(group(r) for r in rows if r.get("passed"))
        per_arm[arm] = {"n_demo": len(chosen), "n_demo_passed": sum(bool(r["passed"]) for r in chosen),
                        "demo_strata": {k: len(v) for k, v in strata.items()},
                        "n_full_run": len(rows), "n_full_run_passed": sum(bool(r.get("passed")) for r in rows),
                        "full_run_correct_labels": dict(full_cc.most_common())}

    prompts = json.loads((src / "data" / "prompts.json").read_text())
    used = {"baseline": [f"P{i}" for i in range(10)], "ban": [f"B{i}" for i in range(10)]}
    for tmpl in ("chat", "tf"):
        prompts[tmpl] = {TASK: {kind: [{k: v for k, v in p.items() if k != "token_ids"}
                                       for p in prompts[tmpl][TASK][kind] if p["id"] in ids]
                                for kind, ids in used.items()}}
    files["data/prompts.json"] = {"kind": "json", "content": prompts}
    for rel in JSON_FILES:
        files[rel] = {"kind": "json", "content": json.loads((src / rel).read_text())}
    for rel in TEXT_FILES:
        files[rel] = {"kind": "text", "content": (src / rel).read_text()}

    an = json.loads((src / "results" / "analysis.json").read_text())
    cs = an["collapse_screen"]
    reference = {

```
{'notebook_path': '/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_1/art_HF8nK8B_Lqyy/code_demo.ipynb', 'cell_id': 'title', 'edit_mode': 'replace', 'cell_type': 'markdown', 'new_source': '# Did post-training cause Qwen3\'s algorithm collapse? (demo of `method.py`)\n\nAsk Qwen3-8B (chat template, thinking off) for a weighted shortest-path solver and 193 of its 200 correct programs are **Dijkstra**. Ask it for a path on a grid and all 200 are **BFS**. This experiment tests one explanation: *instruction tuning installed that collapse*. It compares **Qwen3-8B-Base** with **Qwen3-8B** in a 2×2 design that crosses the weights with the prompt format:\n\n| | template-free prompt (docstring) | chat-template prompt |\n|---|---|---|\n| **base weights** | `base_tf` | `base_chat` |\n| **instruct weights** | `instruct_tf` | `instruct_chat` |\n\nAll four arms get the same task text, the same 10 prompt paraphrases and the same sampling settings (T = 1.0, top-p 1.0). Every generated program runs on hidden tests (46 for this task). Two labellers then name the algorithm it implements: a runtime-signature labeller and an AST labeller. A blind LLM judge settles disagreements. Diversity is measured as the **Hill number of the correct programs**: `Hill_cc = exp(Shannon entropy of their algorithm labels)`. A value of 1.0 means every correct program uses the same algorithm. A value of 2.0 means two equally common algorithms.\n\nThe pre-registered test is `D_weights = Hill_cc(instruct_chat) − Hill_cc(base_chat)`. Both arms see identical chat tokens, so they differ only in the weights. A fifth arm, `instruct_chat_ban`, adds a ban such as *"Do not use Dijkstra."* to the prompt to show where the model goes instead.\n\n**What `method.py` does.** It is the experiment\'s single entry point. Each GPU phase runs as a subprocess: vLLM generation, labelling, HF activation capture, steering, and a hash-committed forecast. Then `assemble()` collects everything into `method_out.json`. The GPU phases need both 8B models on a GPU (about 2.8 GPU-hours on an NVIDIA L4). This notebook therefore:\n\n1. lists the pipeline steps as a dry run;\n2. runs the CPU part of the `analysis` step on **100 real generated programs**, with the code copied unchanged from `src/stats_utils.py` and `src/analysis.py`. This covers per-arm Hill numbers with paraphrase-cluster bootstrap CIs, permutation χ² tests, the pre-registered verdict, the ban row and the hash check of the committed forecast;\n3. runs `assemble()` to write `method_out.json`;\n4. plots the demo numbers next to the full-run numbers.\n\n**Full-run result on this task (T2, weighted shortest path):** `INSTALLED_BY_POST_TRAINING`, with `D_weights = −0.93 [−1.38, −0.46]`. Under the same chat tokens the base model is more diverse (Dijkstra 63/90 correct programs, Bellman-Ford 24/90, Hill_cc 2.09) than the instruct model (Dijkstra 193/200, Hill_cc 1.16). Post-training *concentrated* a mode the base model already preferred; it did not invent a new one. The Dijkstra ban raises Hill_cc from 1.16 to 1.99 with no loss in pass rate. The model substitutes Bellman-Ford, which the committed forecast predicted.'}
```

It compares **Qwen3-8B-Base** with **Qwen3-8B** in a 2×2 design that crosses the weights with the prompt format:\\n\\n| | template-free prompt (docstring) | chat-template prompt |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| **base weights** | `base_tf` | `base_chat` |\\n| **instruct weights** | `instruct_tf` | `instruct_chat` |\\n\\nAll four arms get the same task text, the same 10 prompt paraphrases and the same sampling settings (T = 1.0, top-p 1.0). Every generated program runs on hidden tests (46 for this task). Two labellers then name the algorithm it implements: a runtime-signature labeller and an AST labeller. A blind LLM judge settles disagreements. Diversity is measured as the **Hill number of the correct programs**: `Hill_cc = exp(Shannon entropy of their algorithm labels)`. A value of 1.0 means every correct program uses the same algorithm. A value of 2.0 means two equally common algorithms.\\n\\nThe pre-registered test is `D_weights = Hill_cc(instruct_chat) − Hill_cc(base_chat)`. Both arms see identical chat tokens, so they differ only in the weights. A fifth arm, `instruct_chat_ban`, adds a ban such as *\\\"Do not use Dijkstra.\\\"* to the prompt to show where the model goes instead.\\n\\n**What `method.py` does.** It is the experiment's single entry point. Each GPU phase runs as a subprocess: vLLM generation, labelling, HF activation capture, steering, and a hash-committed forecast. Then `assemble()` collects everything into `method_out.json`. The GPU phases need both 8B models on a GPU (about 2.8 GPU-hours on an NVIDIA L4). This notebook therefore:\\n\\n1. lists the pipeline steps as a dry run;\\n2. runs the CPU part of the `analysis` step on **100 real generated programs**, with the code copied unchanged from `src/stats_utils.py` and `src/analysis.py`. This covers per-arm Hill numbers with paraphrase-cluster bootstrap CIs, permutation χ² tests, the pre-registered verdict, the ban row and the hash check of the committed forecast;\\n3. runs `assemble()` to write `method_out.json`;\\n4. plots the demo numbers next to the full-run numbers.\\n\\n**Full-run result on this task (T2, weighted shortest path):** `INSTALLED_BY_POST_TRAINING`, with `D_weights = −0.93 [−1.38, −0.46]`. Under the same chat tokens the base model is more diverse (Dijkstra 63/90 correct programs, Bellman-Ford 24/90, Hill_cc 2.09) than the instruct model (Dijkstra 193/200, Hill_cc 1.16). Post-training *concentrated* a mode the base model already preferred; it did not invent a new one. The Dijkstra ban raises Hill_cc from 1.16 to 1.99 with no loss in pass rate. The model substitutes Bellman-Ford, which the committed forecast predicted.\"\n  }\n ],\n \"metadata\": {\n  \"kernelspec\": {\n   \"display_name\": \"Python 3\",\n   \"language\": \"python\",\n   \"name\": \"python3\"\n  },\n  \"language_info\": {\n   \"name\": \"python\"\n  }\n },\n \"nbformat\": 4,\n \"nbformat_minor\": 5\n}", "updated_file": "{\n \"cells\": [\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"markdown\",\n   \"id\": \"title\",\n   \"metadata\": {},\n   \"source\": \"# Did post-training cause Qwen3's algorithm collapse? ```
{'notebook_path': '/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_1/art_HF8nK8B_Lqyy/code_demo.ipynb', 'cell_id': '20ad585b', 'edit_mode': 'insert', 'cell_type': 'markdown', 'new_source': "## Demo data\n\n`mini_demo_data.json` holds **100 real programs** for one task, **T2**: write `solve(n, edges, s, t)` that returns the shortest-path distance in a weighted directed graph. The programs come from five arms: `base_tf` 25, `base_chat` 25, `instruct_tf` 15, `instruct_chat` 20 and `instruct_chat_ban` 15. Each row is an original labelled row from `runs/labelled_*.jsonl`, with the generated text, the extracted program, the hidden-test result and the runtime, AST, judge and final labels.\n\nThe file also carries the small files that `method.py` and the analysis read from disk: prompts, the hidden tests, results files of the GPU phases, and the hash-committed forecast. Finally, `full_run_reference` holds the full-run numbers so the notebook can compare them with the demo.\n\n**How the subset was chosen** (`make_mini_demo_data.py`, seed 0). For each arm the script takes a fixed number of correct programs per algorithm label, chosen so the arm's mix of correct programs stays close to the full run. It then adds failing programs in proportion to the arm's failing-label mix, spreading picks round-robin over the prompt paraphrases. Hill_cc counts correct programs only, so these quotas keep it meaningful at n = 11–20 per arm. The base arms therefore have **more passing programs than in the full run** (full-run pass rates: `base_tf` 8 %, `base_chat` 30 %), and **pass rates in this demo are not representative**."}
```

It compares **Qwen3-8B-Base** with **Qwen3-8B** in a 2×2 design that crosses the weights with the prompt format:\\n\\n| | template-free prompt (docstring) | chat-template prompt |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| **base weights** | `base_tf` | `base_chat` |\\n| **instruct weights** | `instruct_tf` | `instruct_chat` |\\n\\nAll four arms get the same task text, the same 10 prompt paraphrases and the same sampling settings (T = 1.0, top-p 1.0). Every generated program runs on hidden tests (46 for this task). Two labellers then name the algorithm it implements: a runtime-signature labeller and an AST labeller. A blind LLM judge settles disagreements. Diversity is measured as the **Hill number of the correct programs**: `Hill_cc = exp(Shannon entropy of their algorithm labels)`. A value of 1.0 means every correct program uses the same algorithm. A value of 2.0 means two equally common algorithms.\\n\\nThe pre-registered test is `D_weights = Hill_cc(instruct_chat) − Hill_cc(base_chat)`. Both arms see identical chat tokens, so they differ only in the weights. A fifth arm, `instruct_chat_ban`, adds a ban such as *\\\"Do not use Dijkstra.\\\"* to the prompt to show where the model goes instead.\\n\\n**What `method.py` does.** It is the experiment's single entry point. Each GPU phase runs as a subprocess: vLLM generation, labelling, HF activation capture, steering, and a hash-committed forecast. Then `assemble()` collects everything into `method_out.json`. The GPU phases need both 8B models on a GPU (about 2.8 GPU-hours on an NVIDIA L4). This notebook therefore:\\n\\n1. lists the pipeline steps as a dry run;\\n2. runs the CPU part of the `analysis` step on **100 real generated programs**, with the code copied unchanged from `src/stats_utils.py` and `src/analysis.py`. This covers per-arm Hill numbers with paraphrase-cluster bootstrap CIs, permutation χ² tests, the pre-registered verdict, the ban row and the hash check of the committed forecast;\\n3. runs `assemble()` to write `method_out.json`;\\n4. plots the demo numbers next to the full-run numbers.\\n\\n**Full-run result on this task (T2, weighted shortest path):** `INSTALLED_BY_POST_TRAINING`, with `D_weights = −0.93 [−1.38, −0.46]`. Under the same chat tokens the base model is more diverse (Dijkstra 63/90 correct programs, Bellman-Ford 24/90, Hill_cc 2.09) than the instruct model (Dijkstra 193/200, Hill_cc 1.16). Post-training *concentrated* a mode the base model already preferred; it did not invent a new one. The Dijkstra ban raises Hill_cc from 1.16 to 1.99 with no loss in pass rate. The model substitutes Bellman-Ford, which the committed forecast predicted.\"\n  },\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"code\",\n   \"id\": \"a3fc0880\",\n   \"source\": \"import subprocess, sys\\ndef _pip(*a): subprocess.check_call([sys.executable, '-m', 'pip', 'install', '-q', *a])\\n\\n# loguru — NOT pre-installed on Colab, always install (the version the experiment used)\\n_pip('loguru==0.7.3')\\n\\n# numpy, scipy, matplotlib — pre-installed on Colab, install locally only (at Colab's exact versions)\\nif 'google.colab' not in sys.modules:\\n    _pip('numpy==2.0.2', 'scipy==1.16.3', 'matplotlib==3.10.0')\",\n   \"metadata\": {},\n   \"execution_count\": null,\n   \"outputs\": []\n  },\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"code\",\n   \"id\": \"20ad585b\",\n   \"source\": \"from __future__ import annotations\\n\\n# ---- method.py's import block (unchanged)\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport os\\nimport platform\\nimport subprocess\\nimport sys\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\nfrom loguru import logger\\n\\n# ---- imports of the src/ modules inlined below (common.py, stats_utils.py, analysis.py)\\nimport hashlib\\nimport math\\nimport random\\nfrom typing import Iterable\\n\\nimport numpy as np\\n\\n# ---- notebook only: figures\\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\",\n   \"metadata\": {},\n   \"execution_count\": null,\n   \"outputs\": []\n  },\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"markdown\",\n   \"id\": \"5688a43e\",\n   \"source\": \"## Demo data\\n\\n`mini_demo_data.json` holds **100 real programs** for one task, **T2**: write `solve(n, edges, s, t)` that returns the shortest-path distance in a weighted directed graph. The programs come from five arms: `base_tf` 25, `base_chat` 25, `instruct_tf` 15, `instruct_chat` 20 and `instruct_chat_ban` 15. Each row is an original labelled row from `runs/labelled_*.jsonl`, with the generated text, the extracted program, the hidden-test result and the runtime, AST, judge and final labels.\\n\\nThe file also carries the small files that `method.py` and the analysis read from disk: prompts, the hidden tests, results files of the GPU phases, and the hash-committed forecast. Finally, `full_run_reference` holds the full-run numbers so the notebook can compare them with the demo.\\n\\n**How the subset was chosen** (`make_mini_demo_data.py`, seed 0). For each arm the script takes a fixed number of correct programs per algorithm label, chosen so the arm's mix of correct programs stays close to the full run. It then adds failing programs in proportion to the arm's failing-label mix, spreading picks round-robin over the prompt paraphrases. Hill_cc counts correct programs only, so these quotas keep it meaningful at n = 11–20 per arm. The base arms therefore have **more passing programs than in the full run** (full-run pass rates: `base_tf` 8 %, `base_chat` 30 %), and **pass rates in this demo are not representative**.\",\n   \"metadata\": {}\n  }\n ],\n \"metadata\": {\n  \"kernelspec\": {\n   \"display_name\": \"Python 3\",\n   \"language\": \"python\",\n   \"name\": \"python3\"\n  },\n  \"language_info\": {\n   \"name\": \"python\"\n  }\n },\n \"nbformat\": 4,\n \"nbformat_minor\": 5\n}"}
```

Under the same chat tokens the base model is more diverse (Dijkstra 63/90 correct programs, Bellman-Ford 24/90, Hill_cc 2.09) than the instruct model (Dijkstra 193/200, Hill_cc 1.16). Post-training *concentrated* a mode the base model already preferred; it did not invent a new one. The Dijkstra ban raises Hill_cc from 1.16 to 1.99 with no loss in pass rate. The model substitutes Bellman-Ford, which the committed forecast predicted.\"\n  },\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"code\",\n   \"id\": \"a3fc0880\",\n   \"source\": \"import subprocess, sys\\ndef _pip(*a): subprocess.check_call([sys.executable, '-m', 'pip', 'install', '-q', *a])\\n\\n# loguru — NOT pre-installed on Colab, always install (the version the experiment used)\\n_pip('loguru==0.7.3')\\n\\n# numpy, scipy, matplotlib — pre-installed on Colab, install locally only (at Colab's exact versions)\\nif 'google.colab' not in sys.modules:\\n    _pip('numpy==2.0.2', 'scipy==1.16.3', 'matplotlib==3.10.0')\",\n   \"metadata\": {},\n   \"execution_count\": null,\n   \"outputs\": []\n  },\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"code\",\n   \"id\": \"20ad585b\",\n   \"source\": \"from __future__ import annotations\\n\\n# ---- method.py's import block (unchanged)\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport os\\nimport platform\\nimport subprocess\\nimport sys\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\nfrom loguru import logger\\n\\n# ---- imports of the src/ modules inlined below (common.py, stats_utils.py, analysis.py)\\nimport hashlib\\nimport math\\nimport random\\nfrom typing import Iterable\\n\\nimport numpy as np\\n\\n# ---- notebook only: figures\\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\",\n   \"metadata\": {},\n   \"execution_count\": null,\n   \"outputs\": []\n  },\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"markdown\",\n   \"id\": \"5688a43e\",\n   \"source\": \"## Demo data\\n\\n`mini_demo_data.json` holds **100 real programs** for one task, **T2**: write `solve(n, edges, s, t)` that returns the shortest-path distance in a weighted directed graph. The programs come from five arms: `base_tf` 25, `base_chat` 25, `instruct_tf` 15, `instruct_chat` 20 and `instruct_chat_ban` 15. Each row is an original labelled row from `runs/labelled_*.jsonl`, with the generated text, the extracted program, the hidden-test result and the runtime, AST, judge and final labels.\\n\\nThe file also carries the small files that `method.py` and the analysis read from disk: prompts, the hidden tests, results files of the GPU phases, and the hash-committed forecast. Finally, `full_run_reference` holds the full-run numbers so the notebook can compare them with the demo.\\n\\n**How the subset was chosen** (`make_mini_demo_data.py`, seed 0). For each arm the script takes a fixed number of correct programs per algorithm label, chosen so the arm's mix of correct programs stays close to the full run. It then adds failing programs in proportion to the arm's failing-label mix, spreading picks round-robin over the prompt paraphrases. Hill_cc counts correct programs only, so these quotas keep it meaningful at n = 11–20 per arm. The base arms therefore have **more passing programs than in the full run** (full-run pass rates: `base_tf` 8 %, `base_chat` 30 %), and **pass rates in this demo are not representative**.\",\n   \"metadata\": {}\n  },\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"code\",\n   \"id\": \"cd3489f9\",\n   \"source\": \"GITHUB_DATA_URL = \\\"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ai-inventor-papers/ai-invention-295f8e-prompt-bans-substitute-not-just-subtract/fork/run_jSassd-YWLVC/round-1/experiment-5/demo/mini_demo_data.json\\\"\\nimport http.client\\nimport json\\nimport urllib.request\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\ndef load_data():\\n    try:\\n        with urllib.request.urlopen(GITHUB_DATA_URL, timeout=15) as response:\\n            return json.loads(response.read().decode())\\n    # Network errors, timeouts, a placeholder URL, bad JSON, a cut-off response.\\n    except (OSError, ValueError, http.client.HTTPException) as e:\\n        print(\\\"GitHub data unavailable, using local mini_demo_data.json:\\\", e)\\n    local = Path(\\\"mini_demo_data.json\\\")\\n    if local.exists(): return json.loads(local.read_text())\\n    raise FileNotFoundError(\\\"Could not load mini_demo_data.json\\\")\",\n   \"metadata\": {},\n   \"execution_count\": null,\n   \"outputs\": []\n  }\n ],\n \"metadata\": {\n  \"kernelspec\": {\n   \"display_name\": \"Python 3\",\n   \"language\": \"python\",\n   \"name\": \"python3\"\n  },\n  \"language_info\": {\n   \"name\": \"python\"\n  }\n },\n \"nbformat\": 4,\n \"nbformat_minor\": 5\n}", "updated_file": "{\n \"cells\": [\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"markdown\",\n   \"id\": \"title\",\n   \"metadata\": {},\n   \"source\": \"# Did post-training cause Qwen3's algorithm collapse? (demo of `method.py`)\\n\\nAsk Qwen3-8B (chat template, thinking off) for a weighted shortest-path solver and 193 of its 200 correct programs are **Dijkstra**. Ask it for a path on a grid and all 200 are **BFS**. This experiment tests one explanation: *instruction tuning installed that collapse*. It compares **Qwen3-8B-Base** with **Qwen3-8B** in a 2×2 design that crosses the weights with the prompt format:\\n\\n| | template-free prompt (docstring) | chat-template prompt |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| **base weights** | `base_tf` | `base_chat` |\\n| **instruct weights** | `instruct_tf` | `instruct_chat` |\\n\\nAll four arms get the same task text, the same 10 prompt paraphrases and the same sampling settings (T = 1.0, top-p 1.0). Every generated program runs on hidden tests (46 for this task). Two labellers then name the algorithm it implements: a runtime-signature labeller and an AST labeller. A blind LLM judge settles disagreements. Diversity is measured as the **Hill number of the correct programs**: `Hill_cc = exp(Shannon entropy of their algorithm labels)`. A value of 1.0 means every correct program uses the same algorithm. A value of 2.0 means two equally common algorithms.\\n\\nThe pre-registered test is `D_weights = Hill_cc(instruct_chat) − Hill_cc(base_chat)`. Both arms see identical chat tokens, so they differ only in the weights. A fifth arm, `instruct_chat_ban`, adds a ban such as *\\\"Do not use Dijkstra.\\\"* to the prompt to show where the model goes instead.\\n\\n**What `method.py` does.** It is the experiment's single entry point. Each GPU phase runs as a subprocess: vLLM generation, labelling, HF activation capture, steering, and a hash-committed forecast. Then `assemble()` collects everything into `method_out.json`. The GPU phases need both 8B models on a GPU (about 2.8 GPU-hours on an NVIDIA L4). This notebook therefore:\\n\\n1. lists the pipeline steps as a dry run;\\n2. runs the CPU part of the `analysis` step on **100 real generated programs**, with the code copied unchanged from `src/stats_utils.py` and `src/analysis.py`. This covers per-arm Hill numbers with paraphrase-cluster bootstrap CIs, permutation χ² tests, the pre-registered verdict, the ban row and the hash check of the committed forecast;\\n3. runs `assemble()` to write `method_out.json`;\\n4. plots the demo numbers next to the full-run numbers.\\n\\n**Full-run result on this task (T2, weighted shortest path):** `INSTALLED_BY_POST_TRAINING`, with `D_weights = −0.93 [−1.38, −0.46]`. Under the same chat tokens the base model is more diverse (Dijkstra 63/90 correct programs, Bellman-Ford 24/90, Hill_cc 2.09) than the instruct model (Dijkstra 193/200, Hill_cc 1.16). Post-training *concentrated* a mode the base model already preferred; it did not invent a new one. The Dijkstra ban raises Hill_cc from 1.16 to 1.99 with no loss in pass rate. The model substitutes Bellman-Ford, which the committed forecast predicted.\"\n  },\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"code\",\n   \"id\": \"a3fc0880\",\n   \"source\": \"import subprocess, sys\\ndef _pip(*a): subprocess.check_call([sys.executable, '-m', 'pip', 'install', '-q', *a])\\n\\n# loguru — NOT pre-installed on Colab, always install (the version the experiment used)\\n_pip('loguru==0.7.3')\\n\\n# numpy, scipy, matplotlib — pre-installed on Colab, install locally only (at Colab's exact versions)\\nif 'google.colab' not in sys.modules:\\n    _pip('numpy==2.0.2', 'scipy==1.16.3', 'matplotlib==3.10.0')\",\n   \"metadata\": {},\n   \"execution_count\": null,\n   \"outputs\": []\n  },\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"code\",\n   \"id\": \"20ad585b\",\n   \"source\": \"from __future__ import annotations\\n\\n# ---- method.py's import block (unchanged)\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport os\\nimport platform\\nimport subprocess\\nimport sys\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\nfrom loguru import logger\\n\\n# ---- imports of the src/ modules inlined below (common.py, stats_utils.py, analysis.py)\\nimport hashlib\\nimport math\\nimport random\\nfrom typing import Iterable\\n\\nimport numpy as np\\n\\n# ---- notebook only: figures\\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\",\n   \"metadata\": {},\n   \"execution_count\": null,\n   \"outputs\": []\n  },\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"markdown\",\n   \"id\": \"5688a43e\",\n   \"source\": \"## Demo data\\n\\n`mini_demo_data.json` holds **100 real programs** for one task, **T2**: write `solve(n, edges, s, t)` that returns the shortest-path distance in a weighted directed graph. The model substitutes Bellman-Ford, which the committed forecast predicted.\"\n  },\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"code\",\n   \"id\": \"a3fc0880\",\n   \"source\": \"import subprocess, sys\\ndef _pip(*a): subprocess.check_call([sys.executable, '-m', 'pip', 'install', '-q', *a])\\n\\n# loguru — NOT pre-installed on Colab, always install (the version the experiment used)\\n_pip('loguru==0.7.3')\\n\\n# numpy, scipy, matplotlib — pre-installed on Colab, install locally only (at Colab's exact versions)\\nif 'google.colab' not in sys.modules:\\n    _pip('numpy==2.0.2', 'scipy==1.16.3', 'matplotlib==3.10.0')\",\n   \"metadata\": {},\n   \"execution_count\": null,\n   \"outputs\": []\n  },\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"code\",\n   \"id\": \"20ad585b\",\n   \"source\": \"from __future__ import annotations\\n\\n# ---- method.py's import block (unchanged)\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport os\\nimport platform\\nimport subprocess\\nimport sys\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\nfrom loguru import logger\\n\\n# ---- imports of the src/ modules inlined below (common.py, stats_utils.py, analysis.py)\\nimport hashlib\\nimport math\\nimport random\\nfrom typing import Iterable\\n\\nimport numpy as np\\n\\n# ---- notebook only: figures\\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\",\n   \"metadata\": {},\n   \"execution_count\": null,\n   \"outputs\": []\n  },\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"markdown\",\n   \"id\": \"5688a43e\",\n   \"source\": \"## Demo data\\n\\n`mini_demo_data.json` holds **100 real programs** for one task, **T2**: write `solve(n, edges, s, t)` that returns the shortest-path distance in a weighted directed graph. The programs come from five arms: `base_tf` 25, `base_chat` 25, `instruct_tf` 15, `instruct_chat` 20 and `instruct_chat_ban` 15. Each row is an original labelled row from `runs/labelled_*.jsonl`, with the generated text, the extracted program, the hidden-test result and the runtime, AST, judge and final labels.\\n\\nThe file also carries the small files that `method.py` and the analysis read from disk: prompts, the hidden tests, results files of the GPU phases, and the hash-committed forecast. Finally, `full_run_reference` holds the full-run numbers so the notebook can compare them with the demo.\\n\\n**How the subset was chosen** (`make_mini_demo_data.py`, seed 0). For each arm the script takes a fixed number of correct programs per algorithm label, chosen so the arm's mix of correct programs stays close to the full run. It then adds failing programs in proportion to the arm's failing-label mix, spreading picks round-robin over the prompt paraphrases. Hill_cc counts correct programs only, so these quotas keep it meaningful at n = 11–20 per arm. The base arms therefore have **more passing programs than in the full run** (full-run pass rates: `base_tf` 8 %, `base_chat` 30 %), and **pass rates in this demo are not representative**.\",\n   \"metadata\": {}\n  },\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"code\",\n   \"id\": \"cd3489f9\",\n   \"source\": \"GITHUB_DATA_URL = \\\"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ai-inventor-papers/ai-invention-295f8e-prompt-bans-substitute-not-just-subtract/fork/run_jSassd-YWLVC/round-1/experiment-5/demo/mini_demo_data.json\\\"\\nimport http.client\\nimport json\\nimport urllib.request\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\ndef load_data():\\n    try:\\n        with urllib.request.urlopen(GITHUB_DATA_URL, timeout=15) as response:\\n            return json.loads(response.read().decode())\\n    # Network errors, timeouts, a placeholder URL, bad JSON, a cut-off response.\\n    except (OSError, ValueError, http.client.HTTPException) as e:\\n        print(\\\"GitHub data unavailable, using local mini_demo_data.json:\\\", e)\\n    local = Path(\\\"mini_demo_data.json\\\")\\n    if local.exists(): return json.loads(local.read_text())\\n    raise FileNotFoundError(\\\"Could not load mini_demo_data.json\\\")\",\n   \"metadata\": {},\n   \"execution_count\": null,\n   \"outputs\": []\n  },\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"code\",\n   \"id\": \"30cb2b48\",\n   \"source\": \"data = load_data()\\nprint(data[\\\"description\\\"])\\nprint(f\\\"\\\\ndataset: {data['dataset']}   files bundled: {len(data['files'])}\\\")\\nfor arm, s in data[\\\"selection\\\"][\\\"per_arm\\\"].items():\\n    print(f\\\"  {arm:18s} demo: {s['n_demo']:3d} programs ({s['n_demo_passed']:2d} pass)   \\\"\\n          f\\\"full run: {s['n_full_run']:3d} programs ({s['n_full_run_passed']:3d} pass)\\\")\",\n   \"metadata\": {},\n   \"execution_count\": null,\n   \"outputs\": []\n  },\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"markdown\",\n   \"id\": \"7846e759\",\n   \"source\": \"## Configuration\\n\\nAll tunable parameters are set here. `B_BOOT` and `N_PERM` match the original analysis. `ROWS_PER_ARM` caps how many demo programs each arm uses; `None` uses all 100. The full run used 300 / 300 / 100 / 200 / 100 programs per arm, which needs the GPU pipeline to generate. `DEMO_TASKS` replaces the hard-coded `(\\\"t1\\\", \\\"t2\\\")` loops of the original, because the demo data covers T2 only.\",\n   \"metadata\": {}\n  }\n ],\n \"metadata\": {\n  \"kernelspec\": {\n   \"display_name\": \"Python 3\",\n   \"language\": \"python\",\n   \"name\": \"python3\"\n  },\n  \"language_info\": {\n   \"name\": \"python\"\n  }\n },\n \"nbformat\": 4,\n \"nbformat_minor\": 5\n}"}

(demo of `method.py`)\\n\\nAsk Qwen3-8B (chat template, thinking off) for a weighted shortest-path solver and 193 of its 200 correct programs are **Dijkstra**. Ask it for a path on a grid and all 200 are **BFS**. This experiment tests one explanation: *instruction tuning installed that collapse*. It compares **Qwen3-8B-Base** with **Qwen3-8B** in a 2×2 design that crosses the weights with the prompt format:\\n\\n| | template-free prompt (docstring) | chat-template prompt |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| **base weights** | `base_tf` | `base_chat` |\\n| **instruct weights** | `instruct_tf` | `instruct_chat` |\\n\\nAll four arms get the same task text, the same 10 prompt paraphrases and the same sampling settings (T = 1.0, top-p 1.0). Every generated program runs on hidden tests (46 for this task). Two labellers then name the algorithm it implements: a runtime-signature labeller and an AST labeller. A blind LLM judge settles disagreements. Diversity is measured as the **Hill number of the correct programs**: `Hill_cc = exp(Shannon entropy of their algorithm labels)`. A value of 1.0 means every correct program uses the same algorithm. A value of 2.0 means two equally common algorithms.\\n\\nThe pre-registered test is `D_weights = Hill_cc(instruct_chat) − Hill_cc(base_chat)`. Both arms see identical chat tokens, so they differ only in the weights. A fifth arm, `instruct_chat_ban`, adds a ban such as *\\\"Do not use Dijkstra.\\\"* to the prompt to show where the model goes instead.\\n\\n**What `method.py` does.** It is the experiment's single entry point. Each GPU phase runs as a subprocess: vLLM generation, labelling, HF activation capture, steering, and a hash-committed forecast. Then `assemble()` collects everything into `method_out.json`. The GPU phases need both 8B models on a GPU (about 2.8 GPU-hours on an NVIDIA L4). This notebook therefore:\\n\\n1. lists the pipeline steps as a dry run;\\n2. runs the CPU part of the `analysis` step on **100 real generated programs**, with the code copied unchanged from `src/stats_utils.py` and `src/analysis.py`. This covers per-arm Hill numbers with paraphrase-cluster bootstrap CIs, permutation χ² tests, the pre-registered verdict, the ban row and the hash check of the committed forecast;\\n3. runs `assemble()` to write `method_out.json`;\\n4. plots the demo numbers next to the full-run numbers.\\n\\n**Full-run result on this task (T2, weighted shortest path):** `INSTALLED_BY_POST_TRAINING`, with `D_weights = −0.93 [−1.38, −0.46]`. Under the same chat tokens the base model is more diverse (Dijkstra 63/90 correct programs, Bellman-Ford 24/90, Hill_cc 2.09) than the instruct model (Dijkstra 193/200, Hill_cc 1.16). Post-training *concentrated* a mode the base model already preferred; it did not invent a new one. The Dijkstra ban raises Hill_cc from 1.16 to 1.99 with no loss in pass rate. (demo of `method.py`)\\n\\nAsk Qwen3-8B (chat template, thinking off) for a weighted shortest-path solver and 193 of its 200 correct programs are **Dijkstra**. Ask it for a path on a grid and all 200 are **BFS**. This experiment tests one explanation: *instruction tuning installed that collapse*. It compares **Qwen3-8B-Base** with **Qwen3-8B** in a 2×2 design that crosses the weights with the prompt format:\\n\\n| | template-free prompt (docstring) | chat-template prompt |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| **base weights** | `base_tf` | `base_chat` |\\n| **instruct weights** | `instruct_tf` | `instruct_chat` |\\n\\nAll four arms get the same task text, the same 10 prompt paraphrases and the same sampling settings (T = 1.0, top-p 1.0). Every generated program runs on hidden tests (46 for this task). Two labellers then name the algorithm it implements: a runtime-signature labeller and an AST labeller. A blind LLM judge settles disagreements. Diversity is measured as the **Hill number of the correct programs**: `Hill_cc = exp(Shannon entropy of their algorithm labels)`. A value of 1.0 means every correct program uses the same algorithm. A value of 2.0 means two equally common algorithms.\\n\\nThe pre-registered test is `D_weights = Hill_cc(instruct_chat) − Hill_cc(base_chat)`. Both arms see identical chat tokens, so they differ only in the weights. A fifth arm, `instruct_chat_ban`, adds a ban such as *\\\"Do not use Dijkstra.\\\"* to the prompt to show where the model goes instead.\\n\\n**What `method.py` does.** It is the experiment's single entry point. Each GPU phase runs as a subprocess: vLLM generation, labelling, HF activation capture, steering, and a hash-committed forecast. Then `assemble()` collects everything into `method_out.json`. The GPU phases need both 8B models on a GPU (about 2.8 GPU-hours on an NVIDIA L4). This notebook therefore:\\n\\n1. lists the pipeline steps as a dry run;\\n2. runs the CPU part of the `analysis` step on **100 real generated programs**, with the code copied unchanged from `src/stats_utils.py` and `src/analysis.py`. This covers per-arm Hill numbers with paraphrase-cluster bootstrap CIs, permutation χ² tests, the pre-registered verdict, the ban row and the hash check of the committed forecast;\\n3. runs `assemble()` to write `method_out.json`;\\n4. plots the demo numbers next to the full-run numbers.\\n\\n**Full-run result on this task (T2, weighted shortest path):** `INSTALLED_BY_POST_TRAINING`, with `D_weights = −0.93 [−1.38, −0.46]`. Under the same chat tokens the base model is more diverse (Dijkstra 63/90 correct programs, Bellman-Ford 24/90, Hill_cc 2.09) than the instruct model (Dijkstra 193/200, Hill_cc 1.16). Post-training *concentrated* a mode the base model already preferred; it did not invent a new one. The Dijkstra ban raises Hill_cc from 1.16 to 1.99 with no loss in pass rate. Instead of rewriting those functions to take in-memory data, this cell writes the bundled files back to their original paths under `WS`, and the original code then runs unchanged. Each arm's rows are cut to `ROWS_PER_ARM`; the rows are pre-ordered so that any prefix is still stratified. The forecast file is written byte-exact, because the analysis checks its sha256 against the commit log."}
```

### [90] TOOL CALL — NotebookEdit · 2026-10-26 09:20:06 UTC

```
{'notebook_path': '/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_1/art_HF8nK8B_Lqyy/code_demo.ipynb', 'cell_id': '682bc958', 'edit_mode': 'insert', 'cell_type': 'markdown', 'new_source': '## The pipeline (`method.py`)\n\n`python method.py --all` runs the steps below in `ORDER`, each as a subprocess so that vLLM and HF never sit on the GPU together, and then calls `assemble()`. The run is resumable: finished requests and rows are skipped. `--step <name>` runs a single step, and `--assemble` rebuilds the outputs from the results on disk.\n\n| step | what it does | needs |\n|---|---|---|\n| `prompts` | builds the prompts: 50 neutral paraphrases per task (P0–P9 are sampled) and 10 ban phrasings, each in chat-template and template-free form | CPU |\n| `tests` | hidden tests (47 for T1, 46 for T2) and reference solutions | CPU |\n| `calibrate` | per-test time limits from the reference solutions: max(1 s, 10× the reference time) | CPU |\n| `gate` | labeller gate: the runtime and AST labellers must recognise 25 reference programs | CPU |\n| `genA`, `genB` | vLLM sampling of the base arms (A) and instruct arms (B) | GPU |\n| `label` | runs every program on the hidden tests; labels it (runtime + AST, LLM judge on disagreement) | CPU + judge API |\n| `acts` | residual states at the last prompt token for both models → the instruct-minus-base direction δ per layer | GPU |\n| `predict` | commits a forecast of where the instruct model lands under the ban, from base-model counts (sha256 + git) | CPU |\n| `genC` | instruct model under the ban prompt (arm `instruct_chat_ban`) | GPU |\n| `steerS`, `steerR`, `steerX`, `steerXNC` | HF steering: subtract c·δ over layers 8–28, norm-matched random controls, single-layer and R-only variants | GPU |\n| `genAEXT` | extends the base arms to N = 300 | GPU |\n| `analysis` | statistics, verdicts, BUY / PREDICT rows, figures → `results/analysis.json` | CPU (+ judge API for `--second_judge`) |\n| `report` | README tables | CPU |\n\nThe next cell is the original code. `setup_logging("method")` comes from `main()` and sends log lines to the cell output and to `logs/method.log`.'}
```

### [101] TOOL CALL — NotebookEdit · 2026-10-26 09:20:47 UTC

```
{'notebook_path': '/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_1/art_HF8nK8B_Lqyy/code_demo.ipynb', 'cell_id': '6787a0b6', 'edit_mode': 'insert', 'cell_type': 'code', 'new_source': 'def lib_versions() -> dict:\n    out = {"python": platform.python_version()}\n    for mod in ("torch", "vllm", "transformers", "numpy", "scipy", "openai"):\n        try:\n            out[mod] = __import__(mod).__version__\n        except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001 - version probe only\n            out[mod] = f"unavailable ({type(e).__name__})"\n    return out\n\n\ndef hardware() -> dict:\n    hw = {"gpu": None, "cpu_quota": None, "ram_limit_gb": None}\n    try:\n        hw["gpu"] = subprocess.run(["nvidia-smi", "--query-gpu=name,memory.total,driver_version", "--format=csv,noheader"],\n                                   capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=20).stdout.strip()\n    except (OSError, subprocess.SubprocessError):\n        pass\n    try:\n        q = int(Path("/sys/fs/cgroup/cpu/cpu.cfs_quota_us").read_text())\n        p = int(Path("/sys/fs/cgroup/cpu/cpu.cfs_period_us").read_text())\n        hw["cpu_quota"] = q / p if q > 0 else None\n        hw["ram_limit_gb"] = int(Path("/sys/fs/cgroup/memory/memory.limit_in_bytes").read_text()) / 1e9\n    except (OSError, ValueError):\n        pass\n    return hw\n\n\ndef clean(x):\n    """Strict JSON: NaN/inf -> None, tuples -> lists, recursively."""\n    import math as _m\n    if isinstance(x, float):\n        return None if (_m.isnan(x) or _m.isinf(x)) else x\n    if isinstance(x, dict):\n        return {str(k): clean(v) for k, v in x.items()}\n    if isinstance(x, (list, tuple)):\n        return [clean(v) for v in x]\n    return x\n\n\ndef gpu_cost() -> dict:\n    """GPU time from time_log.json: sum of per-phase reported seconds and the wall-clock span of GPU phases."""\n    import datetime as _dt\n    tl = jload(WS / "time_log.json", []) or []\n    secs = sum(float(e.get("seconds", 0) or 0) for e in tl if str(e.get("event", "")).endswith("_end"))\n    ts = [_dt.datetime.strptime(e["utc"], "%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ") for e in tl\n          if any(str(e.get("event", "")).startswith(p) for p in ("gen_", "acts_", "steer_"))]\n    span = (max(ts) - min(ts)).total_seconds() if ts else 0.0\n    return {"gpu_hours_phase_sum": round(secs / 3600, 2), "gpu_wall_span_hours": round(span / 3600, 2),\n            "gpu": "1x NVIDIA L4 23 GB"}\n\n\ndef jload(p: Path, default=None):\n    try:\n        return json.loads(p.read_text())\n    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError):\n        return default\n\n\ndef all_labelled() -> list[dict]:\n    rows = []\n    for f in sorted(RUNS.glob("labelled_*.jsonl")):\n        rows.extend(read_jsonl(f))\n    return rows\n\n\nprint("GPU cost of the full run:", gpu_cost())\nprint("labelled programs on disk:", len(all_labelled()))'}

```
{'notebook_path': '/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_1/art_HF8nK8B_Lqyy/code_demo.ipynb', 'cell_id': '6a898d53', 'edit_mode': 'insert', 'cell_type': 'markdown', 'new_source': "## Statistics (`src/stats_utils.py`)\n\nThese are the building blocks of every number in the analysis.\n\n* `hill(counts)`: the Hill number of order 1, `exp(H)`, is the *effective number of algorithms*. One algorithm gives 1.0; k equally common algorithms give k.\n* `ArmData`: one arm as arrays of paraphrase id, label index (−1 = unparseable) and pass flag. `counts(correct_only=True)` gives the label counts behind **Hill_cc**; `correct_only=False` counts every parseable program (**Hill_all**).\n* `boot_stat`: the **paraphrase-cluster bootstrap**. It resamples prompt paraphrases with replacement, *jointly across the arms being compared*, because all arms share P0–P9. It then resamples programs within each chosen paraphrase. A resample is skipped if any arm has fewer than `min_correct` correct programs. The function returns the full-sample estimate and a 95 % percentile CI.\n* `perm_chi2`: a permutation χ² test of whether two arms' label distributions differ. `js` is the Jensen–Shannon divergence and `cohen_kappa` measures agreement between labellers."}
```
