'insert', 'cell_type': 'code', 'new_source': '"""Statistics helpers: Hill numbers, paraphrase-cluster bootstrap, permutation chi-square, JS divergence, kappa."""\n# src/stats_utils.py (unchanged; `wilson` comes from the common.py cell)\n\n\ndef norm_label(lab: str | None, cats: list[str]) -> str | None:\n    if lab is None:\n        return None\n    return lab if lab in cats else "other"\n\n\ndef hill(counts: np.ndarray) -> float:\n    n = counts.sum()\n    if n <= 0:\n        return float("nan")\n    p = counts[counts > 0] / n\n    return float(math.exp(-(p * np.log(p)).sum()))\n\n\ndef js(p_counts: np.ndarray, q_counts: np.ndarray, smooth: float = 0.5) -> float:\n    """Jensen-Shannon divergence (nats). smooth=0 gives the unsmoothed JS (finite: zero cells contribute 0)."""\n    p = (p_counts + smooth) / (p_counts + smooth).sum()\n    q = (q_counts + smooth) / (q_counts + smooth).sum()\n    m = 0.5 * (p + q)\n\n    def kl(a: np.ndarray) -> float:\n        nz = a > 0\n        return float((a[nz] * np.log(a[nz] / m[nz])).sum())\n    return 0.5 * kl(p) + 0.5 * kl(q)\n\n\nclass ArmData:\n    """Per-arm arrays: paraphrase id, sample id, label index (-1 = unparseable), correct flag."""\n\n    def __init__(self, rows: list[dict], cats: list[str], label_key: str = "label"):\n        self.cats = cats + ["other"]\n        self.K = len(self.cats)\n        idx = {c: i for i, c in enumerate(self.cats)}\n        self.pid = np.array([r["paraphrase_id"] for r in rows], dtype=int)\n        self.sid = np.array([r["sample_id"] for r in rows], dtype=int)\n        labs = []\n        for r in rows:\n            lab = r.get(label_key)\n            if not r.get("parse_ok") or lab is None:\n                labs.append(-1)\n            else:\n                labs.append(idx[norm_label(lab, cats)])\n        self.lab = np.array(labs, dtype=int)\n        self.cor = np.array([bool(r.get("passed")) for r in rows], dtype=bool)\n        self.n = len(rows)\n        self.groups = {p: np.where(self.pid == p)[0] for p in np.unique(self.pid)} if self.n else {}\n\n    def counts(self, idx: np.ndarray | None = None, correct_only: bool = True) -> np.ndarray:\n        lab = self.lab if idx is None else self.lab[idx]\n        cor = self.cor if idx is None else self.cor[idx]\n        m = (lab >= 0) & (cor if correct_only else True)\n        return np.bincount(lab[m], minlength=self.K)\n\n    def resample_idx(self, rng: np.random.Generator, pids: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n        parts = []\n        for p in pids:\n            g = self.groups.get(p)\n            if g is None or len(g) == 0:\n                continue\n            parts.append(rng.choice(g, size=len(g), replace=True))\n        return np.concatenate(parts) if parts else np.array([], dtype=int)\n\n\ndef boot_stat(arms: list[ArmData], fn, B: int = 2000, seed: int = 0, min_correct: int = 5) -> dict:\n    """Paraphrase-cluster bootstrap; paraphrase ids are resampled JOINTLY across the given arms.\n    fn(list of index arrays) -> float (nan allowed). Resamples where any arm has < min_correct correct samples are skipped."""\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    all_p = np.unique(np.concatenate([np.array(list(a.groups.keys())) for a in arms if a.n])) if arms else np.array([])\n    vals, skipped = [], 0\n    for _ in range(B):\n        pids = rng.choice(all_p, size=len(all_p), replace=True)\n        idxs = [a.resample_idx(rng, pids) for a in arms]\n        if any(int((a.cor[ix] & (a.lab[ix] >= 0)).sum()) < min_correct for a, ix in zip(arms, idxs)):\n            skipped += 1\n            continue\n        v = fn(idxs)\n        if v is None or (isinstance(v, float) and math.isnan(v)):\n            skipped += 1\n            continue\n        vals.append(v)\n    full = fn([np.arange(a.n) for a in arms])\n    if not vals:\n        return {"est": full, "lo": None, "hi": None, "n_boot": 0, "skipped": skipped}\n    lo, hi = np.percentile(vals, [2.5, 97.5])\n    return {"est": full, "lo": float(lo), "hi": float(hi), "n_boot": len(vals), "skipped": skipped}\n\n\ndef perm_chi2(a_labels: np.ndarray, b_labels: np.ndarray, K: int, n_perm: int = 10000, seed: int = 0) -> dict:\n    a_labels = a_labels[a_labels >= 0]\n    b_labels = b_labels[b_labels >= 0]\n    if len(a_labels) == 0 or len(b_labels) == 0:\n        return {"chi2": None, "p": None, "n_a": int(len(a_labels)), "n_b": int(len(b_labels))}\n    pooled = np.concatenate([a_labels, b_labels])\n    na = len(a_labels)\n\n    def chi2(arr: np.ndarray) -> float:\n        ca = np.bincount(arr[:na], minlength=K).astype(float)\n        cb = np.bincount(arr[na:], minlength=K).astype(float)\n        tot = ca + cb\n        keep = tot > 0\n        ca, cb, tot = ca[keep], cb[keep], tot[keep]\n        ea = tot * na / len(arr)\n        eb = tot * (len(arr) - na) / len(arr)\n        return float(((ca - ea) ** 2 / ea).sum() + ((cb - eb) ** 2 / eb).sum())\n\n    obs = chi2(pooled)\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    ge = 0\n    for _ in range(n_perm):\n        if chi2(rng.permutation(pooled)) >= obs - 1e-12:\n            ge += 1\n    return {"chi2": obs, "p": (ge + 1) / (n_perm + 1), "n_a": int(na), "n_b": int(len(b_labels))}\n\n\ndef cohen_kappa(a: list[str], b: list[str]) -> float | None:\n    if not a:\n        return None\n    cats = sorted(set(a) | set(b))\n    idx = {c: i for i, c in enumerate(cats)}\n    M = np.zeros((len(cats), len(cats)))\n    for x, y in zip(a, b):\n        M[idx[x], idx[y]] += 1\n    n = M.sum()\n    po = np.trace(M) / n\n    pe = (M.sum(0) * M.sum(1)).sum() / (n * n)\n    return float((po - pe) / (1 - pe)) if pe < 1 else 1.0'}

Each contrast is a difference of Hill numbers between two arms, with a paraphrase-joint bootstrap CI:\n\n* `D_weights = Hill(instruct_chat) − Hill(base_chat)`: identical chat tokens, different weights. Negative means post-training reduced diversity.\n* `D_format = Hill(base_chat) − Hill(base_tf)`: base weights, different prompt format.\n* `D_format_instruct`, `D_weights_tf` and the 2×2 `interaction` complete the decomposition. Each is computed for correct programs (`_cc`) and for all parseable programs (`_all`).\n* Permutation χ² tests compare the label distributions of each pair of arms.\n\n`verdict()` applies the pre-registered rules, in this order:\n\n1. **INSTALLED_BY_POST_TRAINING** if `Hill(base_chat) − Hill(instruct_chat) ≥ 0.5` with CI lower bound > 0, **and** the instruct mode's share is ≥ 15 pp higher in `instruct_chat` than in `base_chat`.\n2. **INSTALLED_BY_FORMAT** under the same rule for `base_tf` vs `base_chat`.\n3. **ALREADY_IN_BASE** if `base_tf` has the same mode as `instruct_chat` and `Hill(base_tf) − Hill(instruct_chat)` has a CI upper bound < 0.5.\n4. **MIXED** otherwise.\n\nOnly `n_perm=10000` changed: it now reads `n_perm=N_PERM` from the config cell."}

Hill(b) with paraphrase ids resampled jointly (arms share P0..P9)."""\n    return boot_stat([a, b], lambda ix: hill(a.counts(ix[0], correct_only)) - hill(b.counts(ix[1], correct_only)),\n                     B=B_BOOT, seed=seed, min_correct=5 if correct_only else 0)\n\n\ndef modal_share(ad: ArmData, lab: str, correct_only: bool = True) -> tuple:\n    c = ad.counts(correct_only=correct_only)\n    i = ad.cats.index(lab) if lab in ad.cats else len(ad.cats) - 1\n    return wilson(int(c[i]), int(c.sum()))\n\n\ndef verdict(task: str, A: dict[str, "ArmData"], correct_only: bool) -> dict:\n    """Pre-registered per-task verdict."""\n    tag = "cc" if correct_only else "all"\n    ic, bc, bt = A["instruct_chat"], A["base_chat"], A["base_tf"]\n    d_w = diff_ci(bc, ic, correct_only, 11)    # base_chat - instruct_chat (weights; >0 means post-training collapsed)\n    d_f = diff_ci(bt, bc, correct_only, 12)    # base_tf - base_chat (format)\n    d_tf_ic = diff_ci(bt, ic, correct_only, 13)\n    inst_modal = ic.cats[int(np.argmax(ic.counts(correct_only=correct_only)))]\n    bt_modal = bt.cats[int(np.argmax(bt.counts(correct_only=correct_only)))]\n    bc_modal = bc.cats[int(np.argmax(bc.counts(correct_only=correct_only)))]\n    sh_ic = modal_share(ic, inst_modal, correct_only)[0]\n    sh_bc_same = modal_share(bc, inst_modal, correct_only)[0]\n    sh_bt_bcmodal = modal_share(bt, bc_modal, correct_only)[0]\n    sh_bc_bcmodal = modal_share(bc, bc_modal, correct_only)[0]\n    weights_bar = (d_w["est"] >= 0.5 and d_w["lo"] is not None and d_w["lo"] > 0 and (sh_ic - sh_bc_same) >= 0.15)\n    format_bar = (d_f["est"] >= 0.5 and d_f["lo"] is not None and d_f["lo"] > 0 and (sh_bc_bcmodal - sh_bt_bcmodal) >= 0.15)\n    already = (bt_modal == inst_modal and d_tf_ic["hi"] is not None and d_tf_ic["hi"] < 0.5)\n    if weights_bar:\n        v = "INSTALLED_BY_POST_TRAINING"\n    elif format_bar:\n        v = "INSTALLED_BY_FORMAT"\n    elif already:\n        v = "ALREADY_IN_BASE"\n    else:\n        v = "MIXED"\n    return {"hill_variant": tag, "verdict": v,\n            "hill_base_chat_minus_instruct_chat": d_w, "hill_base_tf_minus_base_chat": d_f,\n            "hill_base_tf_minus_instruct_chat": d_tf_ic,\n            "instruct_modal": inst_modal, "base_tf_modal": bt_modal, "base_chat_modal": bc_modal,\n            "instruct_modal_share": sh_ic, "base_chat_share_of_instruct_modal": sh_bc_same,\n            "modal_share_gap_pp_weights": 100 * (sh_ic - sh_bc_same),\n            "weights_bar_met": bool(weights_bar), "format_bar_met": bool(format_bar), "already_in_base_bar_met": bool(already)}\n\n\ndef collapse_screen(task: str, arms: dict[str, list[dict]], label_key: str = "label", row_filter=None,\n                    with_tests: bool = True) -> dict:\n    cats = CATALOGUE[task]\n    keep = row_filter or (lambda r: True)\n    A = {k: ArmData([r for r in task_rows(arms[k], task) if keep(r)], cats, label_key)\n         for k in ("base_tf", "base_chat", "instruct_chat", "instruct_tf") if k in arms}\n    sizes = {k: {"N": v.n, "n_correct_labelled": int(v.counts().sum())} for k, v in A.items()}\n    out: dict = {"priors": PRIORS[task], "label_key": label_key, "arm_sizes": sizes}\n    if not all(k in A for k in ("base_tf", "base_chat", "instruct_chat")):\n        return {"error": "missing arms"}\n    for co in (True, False):\n        tag = "cc" if co else "all"\n        out[f"D_weights_{tag}"] = diff_ci(A["instruct_chat"], A["base_chat"], co, 21)   # instruct_chat - base_chat\n        out[f"D_format_{tag}"] = diff_ci(A["base_chat"], A["base_tf"], co, 22)          # base_chat - base_tf\n        if "instruct_tf" in A:\n            out[f"D_format_instruct_{tag}"] = diff_ci(A["instruct_chat"], A["instruct_tf"], co, 23)\n            out[f"D_weights_tf_{tag}"] = diff_ci(A["instruct_tf"], A["base_tf"], co, 24)\n            ic, it, bc, bt = A["instruct_chat"], A["instruct_tf"], A["base_chat"], A["base_tf"]\n            out[f"interaction_{tag}"] = boot_stat(\n                [ic, it, bc, bt],\n                lambda ix: (hill(ic.counts(ix[0], co)) - hill(it.counts(ix[1], co))) - (hill(bc.counts(ix[2], co)) - hill(bt.counts(ix[3], co))),\n                B=B_BOOT, seed=25, min_correct=3 if co else 0)\n    tests = {}\n    pairs_t = (("instruct_chat", "base_chat"),) if not with_tests else ()\n    for a, b in pairs_t or (("instruct_chat", "base_chat"), ("base_chat", "base_tf"), ("instruct_chat", "base_tf"),\n                 ("instruct_chat", "instruct_tf"), ("instruct_tf", "base_tf")):\n        if a in A and b in A:\n            for co in (True, False):\n                la = A[a].lab[(A[a].lab >= 0) & (A[a].cor if co else True)]\n                lb = A[b].lab[(A[b].lab >= 0) & (A[b].cor if co else True)]\n                tests[f"{a}_vs_{b}_{\'cc\' if co else \'all\'}"] = perm_chi2(la, lb, A[a].K, n_perm=N_PERM, seed=7)\n    out["permutation_chi2"] = tests\n    out["verdict_cc"] = verdict(task, A, True)\n    out["verdict_all"] = verdict(task, A, False)\n    return out'}

Before the ban arm was sampled, the pipeline wrote `predictions/predict_modal_ban.json` and logged its sha256 and a git commit. The forecast gives, for each predictor, a probability distribution over the surviving algorithms. Its main predictor uses the base model's template-free label counts. The function re-hashes the file and checks it against the log (`hash_match`). It then takes the *compliant* programs of the ban arm: correct programs that avoid Dijkstra. It reports the realised mode and, for each predictor, the top-1 hit, the mean log-probability of the realised labels (`log_score`; uniform ≈ −1.41 on T2) and the JS divergence."}

h0) if not (math.isnan(v["hill_cc"]) or math.isnan(h0)) else None\n            loss = 100 * (p0 - v["pass_rate"][0])\n            rows.append({"task": task, "knob": k, "reference": "steer:none:c=0 (HF S0)", "hill_cc": v["hill_cc"],\n                         "pass_rate": v["pass_rate"][0], "delta_hill_cc": dh, "delta_pass_pp": -loss,\n                         "pass_loss_pp": loss, "efficiency": (dh / max(loss, 1.0)) if dh is not None else None,\n                         "n": v["N"], "n_correct": v["n_correct_labelled"]})\n        for target, key in ((2.0, "price_hill2"), (3.0, "price_hill3")):\n            price = "not reached within the tested range"\n            for (c1, h1, q1), (c2, h2, q2) in zip(curve, curve[1:]):\n                if any(math.isnan(x) for x in (h1, h2)):\n                    continue\n                if (h1 - target) * (h2 - target) <= 0 and h1 != h2:\n                    f = (target - h1) / (h2 - h1)\n                    price = 100 * (p0 - (q1 + f * (q2 - q1)))\n                    break\n            for r in rows:\n                if r["knob"].startswith("steer:sharpen"):\n                    r[key] = price\n    if "instruct_chat_ban" in arms and "instruct_chat" in arms:\n        b = ArmData(task_rows(arms["instruct_chat_ban"], task), cats)\n        a = ArmData(task_rows(arms["instruct_chat"], task), cats)\n        hb, ha = hill(b.counts()), hill(a.counts())\n        pb, pa = b.cor.mean(), a.cor.mean()\n        loss = 100 * (pa - pb)\n        rows.append({"task": task, "knob": f"prompt:{BAN_COND[task]}", "reference": "instruct_chat:baseline (vLLM B1)",\n                     "hill_cc": hb, "pass_rate": float(pb), "delta_hill_cc": hb - ha, "delta_pass_pp": -loss,\n                     "pass_loss_pp": loss, "efficiency": (hb - ha) / max(loss, 1.0), "n": b.n, "n_correct": int(b.counts().sum()),\n                     "price_hill2": "n/a (single setting)", "price_hill3": "n/a (single setting)"})\n    return rows\n\n\ndef predict_rows(task: str, arms: dict) -> list[dict]:\n    f = PRED / "predict_modal_ban.json"\n    if not f.exists() or "instruct_chat_ban" not in arms:\n        return []\n    pred = json.loads(f.read_text())\n    sha_now = sha256_file(f)\n    logged = [json.loads(x) for x in (PRED / "hash_log.txt").read_text().splitlines() if x.strip()]\n    sha_committed = logged[-1]["sha256"] if logged else None\n    ev = pred["events"][task]\n    S = ev["survivors"]\n    rows_c = task_rows(arms["instruct_chat_ban"], task)\n    comp = [r for r in rows_c if r.get("passed") and r.get("label") and norm_label(r["label"], CATALOGUE[task]) != BANNED[task]]\n    labs = [norm_label(r["label"], CATALOGUE[task]) for r in comp]\n    labs = [l if l in S else "other" for l in labs]\n    cats = S + ["other"]\n    cnt = np.array([labs.count(c) for c in cats], dtype=float)\n    realised = None\n    if cnt.sum():\n        order = np.argsort(-cnt)\n        lead = (cnt[order[0]] - (cnt[order[1]] if len(order) > 1 else 0)) / cnt.sum()\n        realised = cats[order[0]] if lead >= 0.15 else "no clear mode"\n    out = []\n    for name, pr in ev["predictors"].items():\n        p = pr["p"]\n        ls = float(np.mean([math.log(p[l]) for l in labs])) if labs else None\n        pvec = np.array([p[c] for c in cats])\n        jsd = None\n        if cnt.sum():\n            q = cnt / cnt.sum()\n            m = 0.5 * (pvec + q)\n            jsd = float(0.5 * (pvec * np.log(pvec / m)).sum() + 0.5 * sum(qi * math.log(qi / mi) for qi, mi in zip(q, m) if qi > 0))\n        out.append({"task": task, "event": ev["event"], "predictor": name, "primary": name == ev["primary"],\n                    "sha256_committed": sha_committed, "sha256_now": sha_now, "hash_match": sha_committed == sha_now,\n                    "n_samples": len(rows_c), "n_compliant": len(comp), "realised_counts": {c: int(x) for c, x in zip(cats, cnt)},\n                    "realised_mode": realised, "top1": pr["top1"],\n                    "top1_hit": (realised == pr["top1"]) if realised not in (None, "no clear mode") else False,\n                    "log_score": ls, "js_to_realised": jsd, "flags": ev.get("flags", [])})\n    return out'}
```

Per arm, it adds the Cohen κ between the runtime and AST labels, an audit of 15 random programs against the judge, and the share of labels each source decided. With `SECOND_JUDGE = True` the original also re-labels 40 audit programs with a second LLM judge; that needs an API key, so it is off here.\n* `judge_sensitivity()` recomputes Hill numbers with the judge's labels in place of the final labels.\n* `sanity_checks()` holds known-answer tests: Hill of one label = 1, Hill of four equal labels = 4, the Wilson interval matches scipy, and a sharp forecast beats a uniform one."}
```

20).proportion_ci(method="wilson")\n    out["wilson_matches_scipy"] = bool(abs(w[1] - ci.low) < 1e-9 and abs(w[2] - ci.high) < 1e-9)\n    p_good = {"a": 0.98 * (0.95 * 1.0 + 0.05 / 2), "b": 0.98 * The `acts` step fed the same 50 chat-templated prompts to both models and stored the residual stream at the read position R, the last prompt token. δ_L = mean(h_instruct − h_base) at layer L is the **instruct-minus-base direction**.\n\n**(a)** The two models\' mean states stay almost parallel (cos ≈ 0.98–1.0). δ is highly reliable (split-half cos ≈ 0.998) and largely shared between the two tasks (cos 0.85–0.92 in the steering band). **(b)** δ is a small fraction of the residual norm and overlaps only partly with the difference measured during generation. The logit lens on −δ at layer 28 promotes answer-format and chat tokens ("Conversation", "Prompt", "chat"), not algorithm names.\n\n**(c)** Every diversity knob tested on T2. Subtracting c·δ over layers 8–28 broke the output format (no correct program at c = 0.5, 1.0 or 1.5), and so did one of the two norm-matched random directions. The single-layer and R-only variants kept or lost correctness without raising Hill_cc much. **The one-line prompt ban gives the largest diversity gain at no pass-rate cost.**'}

The instruct weights put almost all of them on Dijkstra (≈ 1.2). That gives `D_weights` ≈ −0.9, and the pre-registered verdict for T2 is `INSTALLED_BY_POST_TRAINING`. Under the chat template, Dijkstra is already the base model\'s favourite.\n* **The prompt format matters too.** Without the chat template, the base model\'s favourite is Bellman-Ford, and the instruct model is less collapsed (Hill_cc 1.93 vs 1.16 in the full run).\n* **A ban in the prompt substitutes rather than subtracts, and it beats steering.** Under "Do not use Dijkstra", 46 % of the instruct model\'s programs switch to Bellman-Ford and none fail the tests, so Hill_cc rises from 1.16 to 1.99. The hash-committed forecast named Bellman-Ford in advance. Forecasts built from the base model\'s chat-template counts, or from the instruct model\'s own counts, did as well. Subtracting the instruct-minus-base direction did not restore diversity at any tested strength.\n* From 100 programs, the demo recovers the direction and rough size of these effects. The full-run numbers (1,000 T2 programs across these five arms) are the ones to cite.\n\nThe full pipeline (`python method.py --all`) needs the experiment\'s `src/` scripts and a GPU with at least 23 GB of memory.'}
```

The two models\' mean states are nearly parallel at the embeddings (cos 0.998) but drift apart in the middle layers (cos 0.35–0.62 for T2 inside the band).\n\n**(b)** Inside the band, δ is about as large as the base residual itself (‖δ‖ / ‖h_base‖ ≈ 0.85–1.2). Through the logit lens at layer 28, subtracting δ suppresses answer-format tokens (```` ``` ````, "here") and promotes chat and prompt tokens ("Conversation", "Prompt", "chat"). That is consistent with δ encoding *how to answer* rather than *which algorithm to use*.\n\n**(c)** Every diversity knob tested on T2. Subtracting c·δ over layers 8–28 destroyed the output format: no parseable program at c = 0.5, 1.0 or 1.5. The single-layer version at layer 18 kept a 98 % pass rate at c ≤ 0.5 with Hill_cc ≤ 1.1, and broke at c = 1.0. ### [201] TOOL CALL — Write · 2026-10-26 09:31:46 UTC

```
File: /tmp/claude-10001/-ai-inventor-aii-data-runs-run-jSassd-YWLVC-4-gen-paper-repo--4-gen-demo-art-notebook-workspaces-iter-1-art-HF8nK8B-Lqyy/57e481af-50c1-4c61-b158-5b066ad989d8/scratchpad/nbedit.py

"""Apply exact-match string edits to notebook cells by cell id.

  python nbedit.py <notebook.ipynb> <edits.json>
edits.json: [{"id": "<cell id>", "old": "<exact text>", "new": "<replacement>"}, ...]
Every old string must occur exactly once in its cell, otherwise nothing is written.
"""
import json
import sys

nb_path, edits_path = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
nb = json.load(open(nb_path))
edits = json.load(open(edits_path))
cells = {c.get("id"): c for c in nb["cells"]}
for e in edits:
    c = cells.get(e["id"])
    if c is None:
        sys.exit(f"no cell with id {e['id']}")
    src = "".join(c["source"]) if isinstance(c["source"], list) else c["source"]
    n = src.count(e["old"])
    if n != 1:
        sys.exit(f"cell {e['id']}: old string found {n} times: {e['old'][:80]!r}")
    src = src.replace(e["old"], e["new"])
    c["source"] = src.splitlines(keepends=True)
with open(nb_path, "w") as f:
    json.dump(nb, f, indent=1, ensure_ascii=False)
    f.write("\n")
print(f"applied {len(edits)} edit(s) to {nb_path}")
```

new = json.load(open("$S/fullcheck/demo_workspace/results/analysis.json"))
def walk(a, b, path, out):
    if isinstance(a, dict) and isinstance(b, dict):
        for k in a:
            if k in b: walk(a[k], b[k], path + "/" + str(k), out)
            else: out["missing"].append(path + "/" + str(k))
    elif isinstance(a, list) and isinstance(b, list) and len(a) == len(b):
        for i, (x, y) in enumerate(zip(a, b)): walk(x, y, f"{path}[{i}]", out)
    elif isinstance(a, float) and isinstance(b, float):
        out["n"] += 1
        if not ((math.isnan(a) and math.isnan(b)) or a == b): out["mismatch"].append((path, a, b))
    else:
        out["n"] += 1
        if a != b: out["mismatch"].append((path, a, b))
for sec, ka, kb in [("collapse_screen t2", ref["collapse_screen"]["t2"], new["collapse_screen"]["t2"]),
                    ("sensitivity judge t2", ref["collapse_screen_sensitivity"]["t2|judge_labels"], new["collapse_screen_sensitivity"]["t2|judge_labels"]),
                    ("sensitivity planned N t2", ref["collapse_screen_sensitivity"]["t2|planned_N_only"], new["collapse_screen_sensitivity"]["t2|planned_N_only"])] + \
                   [(f"arms {a}|t2", ref["arms"][f"{a}|t2"], new["arms"][f"{a}|t2"]) for a in ("base_tf","base_chat","instruct_tf","instruct_chat","instruct_chat_ban")] + \
                   [("PREDICT t2", [r for r in ref["rows"]["PREDICT"] if r["task"]=="t2"], new["rows"]["PREDICT"]),
                    ("BUY prompt t2", [r for r in ref["rows"]["BUY"] if r["task"]=="t2" and r["knob"].startswith("prompt")], new["rows"]["BUY"])]:
    out = {"n": 0, "mismatch": [], "missing": []}
    walk(ka, kb, "", out)
    print(f"{sec:26s} values compared {out['n']:4d}  mismatches {len(out['mismatch'])}  missing {len(out['missing'])}", out["mismatch"][:3], out["missing"][:3])
print("planned-N verdict (full data, notebook code):", new["collapse_screen_sensitivity"]["t2|planned_N_only"]["verdict_cc"]["verdict"])
EOF

It resamples prompt paraphrases with replacement, *jointly across the arms being compared*, because all arms share P0–P9. It then resamples programs within each chosen paraphrase. A resample is skipped if any arm has fewer than `min_correct` correct programs. The function returns the full-sample estimate and a 95 % percentile CI.\n* `perm_chi2`: a permutation χ² test of whether two arms' label distributions differ. `js` is the Jensen–Shannon divergence and `cohen_kappa` measures agreement between labellers.\n----- [20] c5ad46ac\n## Analysis step 1: load the arms and summarise each one (`src/analysis.py`)\n\n`load_arms()` reads one labelled file per vLLM arm, plus any steering arms. Each contrast is a difference of Hill numbers between two arms, with a paraphrase-joint bootstrap CI:\n\n* `D_weights = Hill(instruct_chat) − Hill(base_chat)`: identical chat tokens, different weights. Negative means post-training reduced diversity.\n* `D_format = Hill(base_chat) − Hill(base_tf)`: base weights, different prompt format.\n* `D_format_instruct`, `D_weights_tf` and the 2×2 `interaction` complete the decomposition. Each is computed for correct programs (`_cc`) and for all parseable programs (`_all`).\n* Permutation χ² tests compare the label distributions of each pair of arms.\n\n`verdict()` applies the pre-registered rules, in this order:\n\n1. **INSTALLED_BY_POST_TRAINING** if `Hill(base_chat) − Hill(instruct_chat) ≥ 0.5` with CI lower bound > 0, **and** the instruct mode's share is ≥ 15 pp higher in `instruct_chat` than in `base_chat`.\n2. **INSTALLED_BY_FORMAT** under the same rule for `base_tf` vs `base_chat`.\n3. **ALREADY_IN_BASE** if `base_tf` has the same mode as `instruct_chat` and `Hill(base_tf) − Hill(instruct_chat)` has a CI upper bound < 0.5.\n4. **MIXED** otherwise.\n\nOnly `n_perm=10000` changed: it now reads `n_perm=N_PERM` from the config cell.\n----- [24] 2a74b9a9\n## Analysis step 3: what does a ban buy, and was it predicted?\n\n* `buy_rows()` scores each diversity knob by how much `Hill_cc` it gains per pass-rate point it costs. In the full run the knobs are steering settings (relative to the hooked c = 0 control) and the **prompt ban** (relative to `instruct_chat`). The demo has no steering arms, so only the prompt-ban row is computed.\n* `predict_rows()` scores the **hash-committed forecast**. The forecast gives, for each predictor, a probability distribution over the surviving algorithms. Its main predictor uses the base model's template-free label counts. The function re-hashes the file and checks it against the log (`hash_match`). It then takes the *compliant* programs of the ban arm: correct programs that avoid Dijkstra. It reports the realised mode and, for each predictor, the top-1 hit, the mean log-probability of the realised labels (`log_score`; uniform ≈ −1.41 on T2) and the JS divergence.\n----- [26] 820a95fd\n## Analysis step 4: labeller QA and sanity checks\n\nThe verdict is only as good as the algorithm labels, so the analysis audits them.\n\n* `labeller_qa()` reports the reference gate from the full run: the AST labeller named all 25 reference programs correctly, and the runtime labeller was never wrong. With `SECOND_JUDGE = True` the original also re-labels 40 audit programs with a second LLM judge; that needs an API key, so it is off here.\n* `judge_sensitivity()` recomputes Hill numbers with the judge's labels in place of the final labels.\n* `sanity_checks()` holds known-answer tests: Hill of one label = 1, Hill of four equal labels = 4, the Wilson interval matches scipy, and a sharp forecast beats a uniform one.\n----- [28] a2f0fe79\n## Run the analysis step\n\nThis is `main()` of `src/analysis.py`, the `analysis` step of the pipeline. It also runs two robustness variants of the screen: one with the judge's labels, and one restricted to the planned sample size (rows not from the N = 300 extension). It writes `results/analysis.json`, which `assemble()` reads next. Changes, each marked in the code:\n\n* the task loops use `DEMO_TASKS` instead of `(\"t1\", \"t2\")`;\n* `--second_judge` becomes `SECOND_JUDGE`;\n* `steering()` is not called. Its arms (HF steering runs) are not in the demo data, so the cell records what the original returns when they are missing: `{\"error\": \"no S0\"}`;\n* `ban_obedience()` is skipped because it needs the `base_tf_ban` arm, and `figures()` is skipped because the notebook draws its own figures at the end;\n* the function returns `res` so the notebook can print it.\n\nThe full-run values of the skipped analyses are in `data[\"full_run_reference\"]` and the experiment's README.\n----- [30] f878743a\n## Assemble `method_out.json` (`method.py`)\n\n`assemble()` is the last thing `method.py --all` does, and it is what `--assemble` runs alone. It does three things:\n\n1. writes `runs/samples.jsonl`, one row per program in the shared schema (`shared_schema()`);\n2. turns every program into an `example` (`example_of()`), pairing the prompt text (`input`) and the generated text (`output`) with the predicted algorithm label and the pass/fail result; the other fields become `metadata_*`;\n3. builds the `metadata` block from the analysis results and the provenance files: models, decoding, library versions, hardware, deviations, the time log, prompt checks, labeller QA, the collapse screen, the sharpening direction, steering, the forecast commit record and costs.\n\nThe output follows the pipeline's `exp_gen_sol_out` schema: `{\"metadata\": ..., \"datasets\": [{\"dataset\": ..., \"examples\": [...]}]}`. The only change is that the task loops use `DEMO_TASKS`. `load_prompts()` is the one-line loader from `src/prompts.py`.\n----- [32] 43d3716b\n## Inspect the output\n\nLet's look at what `method_out.json` contains: the metadata sections, the fields of one example, the hidden-test count and the forecast commit record. Then we print one real prompt and program. The program comes from the ban arm: the instruct model was told not to use Dijkstra, wrote a correct solution anyway, and we print the label each labeller gave it (runtime, AST, judge) and the final label.\n----- [36] eca61e27\n### Figure 1: where the collapse comes from\n\n**(a)** Which algorithm each correct program implements, per arm. The base model is split between Dijkstra and Bellman-Ford. The template-free prompt even makes Bellman-Ford its favourite; the chat template makes it Dijkstra. The instruct weights put almost all of them on Dijkstra (≈ 1.2). That gives `D_weights` ≈ −0.9, and the pre-registered verdict for T2 is `INSTALLED_BY_POST_TRAINING`. ````
File: /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_1/art_HF8nK8B_Lqyy/README.md

# Demo: did post-training cause Qwen3's algorithm collapse?

A runnable notebook for the experiment *"Did post-training cause Qwen3's algorithm collapse?"*, which compares
Qwen3-8B-Base with Qwen3-8B (thinking off) on path-search coding tasks.
Asked for a weighted shortest-path solver, the instruct model writes Dijkstra in 193 of 200 correct programs. The
experiment asks whether instruction tuning installed that collapse, using a 2×2 design that crosses the weights
(base vs instruct) with the prompt format (template-free vs chat template), plus a "Do not use Dijkstra" ban arm.

The experiment's entry point, `method.py`, runs GPU phases (vLLM generation, HF activation capture and steering) and
then `assemble()`s `method_out.json`. The notebook splits that script into cells and lists the GPU steps as a dry run.
It then runs the CPU part of the `analysis` step and `assemble()` on 100 real generated programs, and plots the
results next to the full run. The code from `method.py`, `src/common.py`, `src/stats_utils.py` and `src/analysis.py`
is copied unchanged, apart from a few marked lines (see *Changes to the original code*).

## Layout

| path | what it is |
|---|---|
| `code_demo.ipynb` | the demo notebook, saved with outputs from a clean run (about 30 s on CPU) |
| `mini_demo_data.json` | demo data (1.2 MB). It holds 100 labelled programs for task T2 (weighted directed shortest path) from five arms, the small files `method.py` reads from disk (prompts, hidden tests, GPU-phase results, the hash-committed forecast), and the full-run reference numbers |
| `make_mini_demo_data.py` | builds `mini_demo_data.json` from the experiment folder (deterministic, seed 0) |
| `.gitignore` | ignores `demo_workspace/`, which the notebook creates when run |
| `.aii/manifest.yaml` | storage manifest: nothing here needs a keep/delete decision |

## How to run

**Colab.** Open `code_demo.ipynb` and run all cells. The install cell adds only `loguru`, because Colab already has
numpy, scipy and matplotlib. The data is fetched from this repository's raw GitHub URL.

**Locally** (Python 3.12):

```bash
python3.12 -m venv .venv && .venv/bin/pip install jupyter ipykernel
.venv/bin/jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute code_demo.ipynb --output code_demo.ipynb
```

Run locally, the install cell pins Colab's versions (numpy 2.0.2, scipy 1.16.3, matplotlib 3.10.0) and loguru 0.7.3.
If GitHub is unreachable, the notebook falls back to the local `mini_demo_data.json`. It writes its working files to
`./demo_workspace/`.

**Configuration** (config cell):

* `B_BOOT = 2000` and `N_PERM = 10000` are the original analysis values.
* `ROWS_PER_ARM = None` uses all 100 programs; the minimum that still runs is 5 per arm.
* `RUN_GPU_STEPS = False`. Set it to `True` only inside the full experiment repository on a GPU with at least 23 GB.
* `SECOND_JUDGE = False`. The original analysis uses `True`, which needs an OpenRouter API key.

## What the demo shows

| T2, correct programs | demo (100 programs) | full run (1,000 programs) |
|---|---|---|
| Hill_cc base template-free / base chat / instruct template-free / instruct chat / instruct chat + ban | 2.36 / 2.14 / 1.82 / 1.22 / 2.00 | 2.18 / 2.09 / 1.93 / 1.16 / 1.99 |
| D_weights = Hill_cc(instruct_chat) − Hill_cc(base_chat) | −0.92 [−1.68, +0.23] | −0.93 [−1.38, −0.46] |
| pre-registered verdict (Hill_cc / Hill_all) | MIXED / INSTALLED_BY_POST_TRAINING | INSTALLED_BY_POST_TRAINING / INSTALLED_BY_POST_TRAINING |
| ban "Do not use Dijkstra": Hill_cc change, pass-rate change | +0.78, 0 pp | +0.83, 0 pp |
| hash-committed forecast of the substitute | Bellman-Ford: hit; sha256 matches the commit | Bellman-Ford: hit |

The demo's point estimates track the full run. With only 11–20 correct programs per arm, though, the `D_weights` CI
reaches 0, so the demo's Hill_cc verdict reads MIXED; the experiment saw the same at its planned N, before the base
arms were extended to 300. The notebook also plots the full-run mechanistic results that `method_out.json` carries:
the instruct-minus-base residual direction per layer, and every steering knob against the prompt ban.

**About the subset.** For each arm, `make_mini_demo_data.py` takes a fixed number of correct programs per algorithm
label, so that each arm's mix of correct programs matches the full run. It then adds failing programs in proportion
to the arm's failing-label mix, round-robin over the prompt paraphrases. Passing programs are therefore
over-represented in the base arms (full-run pass rates are 8 % and 30 %), so demo pass rates are not representative.

## Changes to the original code

An AST comparison against the original sources finds 63 copied definitions identical. Three differ, each marked in
the notebook:

* `WS` (the experiment folder) is `./demo_workspace`. The notebook writes the bundled files back to their original
  relative paths there, so the original file-based code runs unchanged.
* `collapse_screen` takes `n_perm=N_PERM`, and `assemble` loops over `DEMO_TASKS = ("t2",)` instead of `("t1", "t2")`.
* `analysis.py`'s `main()` becomes `analysis_main()`. It skips `steering()`, `ban_obedience()` and `figures()`, whose
  arms are not in the demo data, and it returns its results.

**Check.** Run on all 1,000 full-run T2 programs instead of the subset, the notebook's cells reproduce all 749 compared
T2 values of the experiment's stored `results/analysis.json` exactly. These cover the collapse screen, both robustness
variants, the per-arm summaries, and the BUY and PREDICT rows.

## Restoring removed files

Nothing in this folder is marked for deletion; `.aii/manifest.yaml` lists no entries. Two things can be regenerated:

* `demo_workspace/` is created by running the notebook (see *How to run*).
* `mini_demo_data.json` is rebuilt with `python make_mini_demo_data.py --src <experiment folder>`. The experiment folder
  is the parent artifact's directory, the one with `method.py`, `runs/`, `results/`, `data/` and `predictions/`.
````

import ast, json
A = "/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_4VFKidE1bxPG/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_5"
orig = {}
for f in ("method.py", "src/common.py", "src/stats_utils.py", "src/analysis.py", "src/prompts.py"):
    for n in ast.parse(open(f"{A}/{f}").read()).body:
        if isinstance(n, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.ClassDef)): orig.setdefault(n.name, n)
        elif isinstance(n, ast.Assign) and isinstance(n.targets[0], ast.Name): orig.setdefault("=" + n.targets[0].id, n)
nb = json.load(open("code_demo.ipynb"))
t = ast.parse("\n".join("".join(c["source"]) for c in nb["cells"] if c["cell_type"] == "code"))
same = changed = 0; names_changed = []
for n in t.body:
    k = n.name if isinstance(n, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.ClassDef)) else ("=" + n.targets[0].id if isinstance(n, ast.Assign) and isinstance(n.targets[0], ast.Name) else None)
    if k in orig:
        if ast.dump(orig[k]) == ast.dump(n): same += 1
        else: changed += 1; names_changed.append(k)
fn_same = sum(1 for n in t.body if isinstance(n, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.ClassDef)) and n.name in orig and ast.dump(orig[n.name]) == ast.dump(n))
print("identical:", same, "(functions/classes:", fn_same, ") changed:", changed, names_changed)
EOF
```

With only 11–20 correct programs per arm, though, the `D_weights` CI\nreaches 0, so the demo's Hill_cc verdict reads MIXED; the experiment saw the same at its planned N, before the base\narms were extended to 300. The notebook also plots the full-run mechanistic results that `method_out.json` carries:\nthe instruct-minus-base residual direction per layer, and every steering knob against the prompt ban.\n\n**About the subset.** For each arm, `make_mini_demo_data.py` takes a fixed number of correct programs per algorithm\nlabel, so that each arm's mix of correct programs matches the full run. It then adds failing programs in proportion\nto the arm's failing-label mix, round-robin over the prompt paraphrases. Passing programs are therefore\nover-represented in the base arms (full-run pass rates are 8 % and 30 %), so demo pass rates are not representative.\n\n## Changes to the original code\n\nAn AST comparison against the original sources finds 63 copied definitions identical. Three differ, each marked in\nthe notebook:\n\n* `WS` (the experiment folder) is `./demo_workspace`. The notebook writes the bundled files back to their original\n  relative paths there, so the original file-based code runs unchanged.\n* `collapse_screen` takes `n_perm=N_PERM`, and `assemble` loops over `DEMO_TASKS = (\"t2\",)` instead of `(\"t1\", \"t2\")`.\n* `analysis.py`'s `main()` becomes `analysis_main()`. It skips `steering()`, `ban_obedience()` and `figures()`, whose\n  arms are not in the demo data, and it returns its results.\n\n**Check.** Run on all 1,000 full-run T2 programs instead of the subset, the notebook's cells reproduce all 749 compared\nT2 values of the experiment's stored `results/analysis.json` exactly. ### [272] ASSISTANT · 2026-10-26 09:38:41 UTC

```
I've built the demo notebook and its data. On a clean Colab-equivalent environment it runs end to end in 32 seconds (limit 600 s), with no errors and both figures rendered.

**How the demo works.** `method.py` itself only launches GPU jobs (generation with two 8B models, activation capture, steering) and then calls `assemble()`, so it can't run on Colab. The notebook therefore:
- lists the GPU pipeline steps as a dry run;
- runs the CPU part of the analysis step on 100 real labelled programs: diversity (Hill) numbers, bootstrap CIs, the pre-registered verdict, the ban result and the check of the hash-committed forecast;
- runs `assemble()` to write `method_out.json`, then compares the demo numbers with the full run in a table and two figures.

**How faithful the code is.** An automated comparison against the original source files finds 65 definitions identical. Three are changed on purpose, each commented in the notebook:
- the working folder now lives under `./demo_workspace`;
- the permutation-test count comes from the config cell;
- the task loops cover T2 only.

Run on all 1,000 full-run T2 programs instead of the subset, the same cells reproduce all 749 compared values in the experiment's stored `analysis.json` exactly.

**Results.** The demo's estimates track the full run: the key weights effect is −0.92 against −0.93, and each arm's diversity is within 0.2. With only 11–20 correct programs per arm, though, that effect's confidence interval reaches 0. So the demo's main verdict reads MIXED, while the full run reads INSTALLED_BY_POST_TRAINING; the secondary (all-programs) variant does reach INSTALLED_BY_POST_TRAINING. The experiment hit the same limit before it extended the base arms to 300 programs. I kept the rule-chosen subset rather than re-picking programs to force the full-run verdict, and the notebook explains this.

**Things to know:**
- **Data subset:** `mini_demo_data.json` covers one task: T2, weighted shortest path. It deliberately over-samples passing programs in the two base-model arms so the correct-programs-only statistic has enough examples. Demo pass rates are therefore not representative, which the notebook says.
- **Data URL:** the GitHub URL returns 404 until the repo is published. The saved run shows the local-file fallback, which is expected.
- **Scaling:** I tested three settings: 5 programs per arm with 10 resamples, then 10 per arm with 200, then all 100 programs with the original 2,000 bootstrap resamples and 10,000 permutations. The final config uses the original values, so nothing is scaled down. The full 300-program arms would need the GPU pipeline.
- **Todo list:** this session had no todo-tracking tool, so I followed the six todos in order without it. I treated the creativity research brief at the end of the paste as background.

Nothing in the workspace needs a keep/delete decision, so the manifest has no entries; I removed the folder the test runs generated.

Files are in the workspace:
- code_demo.ipynb
- mini_demo_data.json
- make_mini_demo_data.py
- README.md
- .gitignore
- .aii/manifest.yaml
- .terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json
```
